####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\data\_pandas_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal

import cml.data_v1 as cmldata
import pandas as pd
from dlh_ai.utils import HDFSClient
from sqlglot import Expression, parse_one
from tenacity import (
    before_sleep_log,
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_exponential,
)

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PandasEngine:
    connection_name: str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez"

    def __post_init__(self):
        self.hdfs_client = HDFSClient()

    @property
    def cursor(self):
        """Property to get a database cursor for executing queries
        NOTE - This will create a new connection & manually it must be closed after use.
        """
        # TODO - check if this is needed or if we can just use self._create_connection.get_cursor()
        conn = self._create_connection()
        return conn.get_cursor()

    def read_table(self, query: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Read a Iceberg table on Hive OCP into a Pandas DataFrame using provided sql query

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            SQL query to read the table

        Returns
        -------
        pd.DataFrame
            Pandas DataFrame containing the data from the specified SQL table
        """
        # verifying the read query
        self._verify_sql_query(query)

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn:
            with base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
                logger.debug("Executing query to read the table")
                from impala.util import as_pandas

                cursor.execute(query)
                data = as_pandas(cursor)
                logger.info(f"Successfully read {len(data)} rows")

        # NOTE - reading from 'Hive on Tez' also add table name as prefix to column names,
        # so we remove it here for consistency with other engines
        return data.rename(
            columns={col: col.upper().split(".")[-1] for col in data.columns}
        )

    def read_csv_from_hdfs(self, path: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        data = self.hdfs_client.read_csv_file(path)
        return data.to_table().to_pandas(
            split_blocks=True,
            self_destruct=True,  # frees Arrow buffers after conversion
            zero_copy_only=False,
        )

    def read_parquet_from_hdfs(self, path: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        data = self.hdfs_client.read_parquet_table(path)
        return data.to_table().to_pandas(
            split_blocks=True,
            self_destruct=True,  # frees Arrow buffers after conversion
            zero_copy_only=False,
        )

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: pd.DataFrame,
        name: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
        batch_size: int = 5000,
    ):
        """Function to insert data from a Pandas DataFrame into a Iceberg table

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            table data in the form of pandas dataframe
        name : str
            table name to write/append DEPRECATED -
        mode : Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"], optional
            how to write data, by default "append"
        """
        if mode != "append":
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"Mode '{mode}' not yet implemented for Pandas Engine"
            )
        # Prepare Query with Backticks
        # NOTE - splitting by '.' to backtick the schema and table separately: `db`.`table`
        if "." in name:
            db, tbl = name.split(".")
            formatted_table_name = f"`{db}`.`{tbl}`"
        else:
            formatted_table_name = f"`{name}`"

        # Backtick every column name to be safe
        col_names = ", ".join([f"`{c.split('.')[-1]}`" for c in df.columns])
        placeholders = ", ".join(["%s"] * len(df.columns))

        insert_sql_query = (
            f"INSERT INTO {formatted_table_name} ({col_names}) VALUES ({placeholders})"
        )

        no_of_batches = self._calculate_batch_size(df, batch_size)
        logger.info(
            f"Starting to load data into {name} in batches of {batch_size} with total batches: {no_of_batches}"
        )

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn:
            with base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
                try:
                    # cursor loop
                    for idx, offset in enumerate(
                        range(0, len(df), batch_size), start=1
                    ):
                        batch = df.iloc[offset : offset + batch_size]
                        logger.debug(f"inserting batch {idx}: out of {no_of_batches}")
                        cursor.executemany(
                            insert_sql_query, batch.to_dict(orient="records")
                        )
                        logger.debug(
                            f"successfully inserted batch {idx} with {len(batch)} rows."
                        )

                    logger.info(f"Successfully loaded {len(df)} rows.")
                except Exception as e:
                    logger.exception(f"SQL error inserting into {table_name} at batch {idx}")
                    raise

    def save_csv_to_hdfs(
        self,
        df: pd.DataFrame,
        path: str,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """Save a pandas DataFrame as a CSV file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the CSV file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_csv()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.to_csv(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", path)
        logger.info(f"saved pandas dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    def save_parquet(self, df: pd.DataFrame, path: str, **kwargs):
        """Save a pandas DataFrame as a Parquet file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the Parquet file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_parquet()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.to_parquet(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", path)
        logger.info(f"saved pandas dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    @retry(
        stop=stop_after_attempt(5),
        wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, max=10),
        retry=retry_if_exception_type(Exception),
        reraise=True,
        before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    )
    def _create_connection(self):
        """Attempt to create and return the DB base connection (with retries)."""
        # TODO - we should explore connection pooling or reuse to avoid the overhead of creating
        # a new connection for every operation, but for now we will create a new connection each
        # time and rely on retries to handle transient issues.

        # if not hasattr(self, "_base_conn") or self._base_conn is None:
        self._base_conn = cmldata.get_connection(self.connection_name)
        logger.info(f"Successfully established connection: {self.connection_name}")
        return self._base_conn.get_base_connection()

    def _calculate_batch_size(self, df: pd.DataFrame, batch_size) -> int:
        """Calculate total bactches based on batch size & data length."""
        # Simple heuristic: 5000 rows per batch as a starting point
        return len(df) // batch_size + (1 if len(df) % batch_size > 0 else 0)

    # TODO - Explore optimizing connection reuse or pooling using SQLAlchemy + Impala

    def _verify_sql_query(self, query: str) -> Expression:
        return parse_one(query)


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\data\_polars_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal

import cml.data_v1 as cmldata
import polars as pl
from sqlglot import Expression, parse_one
from tenacity import (
    before_sleep_log,
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_exponential,
)

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PolarsEngine:
    connection_name: str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez"

    @property
    def cursor(self):
        """Property to get a database cursor for executing queries
        NOTE - This will create a new connection & manually it must be closed after use.
        """
        # TODO - check if this is needed or if we can just use self._create_connection.get_cursor()
        conn = self._create_connection()
        return conn.get_cursor()

    def read_table(self, query: str, schema_overrides: dict | None = None) -> pl.DataFrame:
        """Read a SQL table into a Polars DataFrame using the provided connection and table name

        Parameters
        ----------
        name : str
            name of the SQL table to read, can include schema (e.g. "schema.table

        Returns
        -------
        pl.DataFrame
            Polars DataFrame containing the data from the specified SQL table
        """
        # verifying the read query
        self._verify_sql_query(query)

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn:
            with base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
                logger.debug("Executing query to read the table")
                data = pl.read_database(
                    query, connection=cursor, schema_overrides=schema_overrides
                )
                logger.info(f"Successfully read {len(data)} rows")

        # NOTE - reading from 'Hive on Tez' also add table name as prefix to column names,
        # so we remove it here for consistency with other engines
        return data.rename({col: col.upper().split(".")[-1] for col in data.columns})

    def read_csv(self, path: str) -> pl.DataFrame:
        # TODO - TO implement it use boto3 S3 route
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Support for reading file format not yet implemented in Polars"
        )

    def read_parquet(self, path: str) -> pl.DataFrame:
        # TODO - TO implement it use boto3 S3 route
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Support for reading file format not yet implemented in Polars"
        )

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: pl.DataFrame,
        name: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
        batch_size: int = 5000,
    ):
        """Function to insert data from a Polars DataFrame into a SQL table

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            table data in the form of polars dataframe
        name : str
            table name to write/append DEPRECATED -
        mode : Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"], optional
            how to write data, by default "append"
        """
        if mode != "append":
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"Mode '{mode}' not yet implemented for Polars Engine"
            )
        # Prepare Query with Backticks
        # NOTE - splitting by '.' to backtick the schema and table separately: `db`.`table`
        if "." in name:
            db, tbl = name.split(".")
            formatted_table_name = f"`{db}`.`{tbl}`"
        else:
            formatted_table_name = f"`{name}`"

        # Backtick every column name to be safe
        col_names = ", ".join([f"`{c.split('.')[-1]}`" for c in df.columns])
        placeholders = ", ".join(["%s"] * len(df.columns))

        insert_sql_query = (
            f"INSERT INTO {formatted_table_name} ({col_names}) VALUES ({placeholders})"
        )

        no_of_batches = self._calculate_batch_size(df, batch_size)
        logger.info(
            f"Starting to load data into {name} in batches of {batch_size} with total batches: {no_of_batches}"
        )

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn:
            with base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
                try:
                    # cursor loop
                    for idx, offset in enumerate(
                        range(0, len(df), batch_size), start=1
                    ):
                        batch = df.slice(offset, batch_size)
                        logger.debug(f"inserting batch {idx}: out of {no_of_batches}")
                        cursor.executemany(insert_sql_query, batch.to_dicts())
                        logger.debug(
                            f"successfully inserted batch {idx} with {len(batch)} rows."
                        )

                    logger.info(f"Successfully loaded {len(df)} rows.")
                except Exception as e:
                    logger.exception(f"SQL error inserting into {table_name} at batch {idx}")
                    raise

    def save_csv_to_hdfs(
        self,
        df: pl.DataFrame,
        path: str,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """Save a Polars DataFrame as a CSV file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the CSV file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_csv()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.write_csv(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", path)
        logger.info(f"saved polars dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    def save_parquet_to_hdfs(self, df: pl.DataFrame, path: str, **kwargs):
        """Save a Polars DataFrame as a Parquet file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the Parquet file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pl.DataFrame.to_parquet()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.write_parquet(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", path)
        logger.info(f"saved polars dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    @retry(
        stop=stop_after_attempt(5),
        wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, max=10),
        retry=retry_if_exception_type(Exception),
        reraise=True,
        before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    )
    def _create_connection(self):
        """Attempt to create and return the DB base connection (with retries)."""
        # TODO - we should explore connection pooling or reuse to avoid the overhead of creating
        # a new connection for every operation, but for now we will create a new connection each
        # time and rely on retries to handle transient issues.

        # if not hasattr(self, "_base_conn") or self._base_conn is None:
        self._base_conn = cmldata.get_connection(self.connection_name)
        logger.info(f"Successfully established connection: {self.connection_name}")
        return self._base_conn.get_base_connection()

    def _calculate_batch_size(self, df: pl.DataFrame, batch_size) -> int:
        """Calculate total bactches based on batch size & data length."""
        # Simple heuristic: 5000 rows per batch as a starting point
        return len(df) // batch_size + (1 if len(df) % batch_size > 0 else 0)

    # TODO - Explore optimizing connection reuse or pooling using SQLAlchemy + Impala

    def _verify_sql_query(self, query: str) -> Expression:
        return parse_one(query)


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\data\_spark_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Literal
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

from pyspark.sql import SparkSession
from pyspark.sql.dataframe import DataFrame
from sqlglot import parse_one

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class SparkEngine:
    """Spark-backed data access and persistence engine.

    Parameters
    ----------
    app_name_prefix : str
        Prefix used while constructing the Spark application name during
        session initialization.
    spark: SparkSession
        Provide already created spark session so that it will be used instead of creating a new one
    """

    app_name_prefix: str | None = None
    spark: SparkSession | None = None

    def __post_init__(self):
        """Initialize the Spark session for the engine instance.

        Notes
        -----
        The Spark application name is created by combining
        ``app_name_prefix`` with the current timestamp in the
        ``Asia/Kolkata`` timezone.
        """
        # FIXME - Below logic of creating spark session logic will work with
        # Notebook session because it can access default-spark.conf but not
        # with CML Jobs
        if self.spark:
            logger.debug(
                f"using existing spark session: {self.spark.sparkContext.appName}"
            )
        else:
            if self.app_name_prefix is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    " No spark session provided neither app name for creating a new one"
                )
            logger.debug("creating new spark session")
            sess_name = f"{self.app_name_prefix}-{datetime.now(ZoneInfo('Asia/Kolkata')).isoformat()}"
            self.spark = SparkSession.builder.appName(sess_name).getOrCreate()
            logger.debug(f"successfully created new spark session: {sess_name}")

    def read_table(self, name: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a table from the Spark catalog.

        Parameters
        ----------
        name : str
            Fully qualified table name to read.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame loaded from the requested table.
        """
        return self.spark.read.table(name)

    def read_csv(self, path: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a CSV dataset from the provided path.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            Filesystem/Ozone path from which the CSV data should be loaded.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame created from the CSV input.
        """
        return self.spark.read.format("csv").option("path", path).load()

    def read_parquet(self, path: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a Parquet dataset from the provided path.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            Filesystem/Ozone path from which the Parquet data should be loaded.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame created from the Parquet input.
        """
        return self.spark.read.format("parquet").option("path", path).load()

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        name: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Persist a DataFrame as an Iceberg table.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        name : str
            Target table name for persistence.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the table.
        """
        df.write.mode(mode).format("iceberg").saveAsTable(name)

    def merge_table(self, df: DataFrame, merge_query: str):
        """Merge a DataFrame into a target Iceberg table using a SQL MERGE statement.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Source Spark DataFrame that will be exposed as a temporary view for
            the merge operation.
        merge_query : str
            SQL MERGE statement used to perform the upsert or merge logic.
        """
        # parsing merge query to validate syntax & extract source name for temp view
        parsed_query = parse_one(merge_query)
        source: str = parsed_query.args.get("using").name

        # creating temp view with source table name provided in merge query
        df.createOrReplaceTempView(source)
        logger.debug(f"created {source} temp view")

        self.spark.sql(merge_query)
        logger.info(
            f"successfully merged into {parsed_query.this.db}.{parsed_query.this.name}"
        )

    def save_csv(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        path: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Write a DataFrame to CSV format.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        path : str
            Destination filesystem path for the CSV output.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the CSV data.
        """
        df.write.mode(mode).format("csv").save(path)

    def save_parquet(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        path: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Write a DataFrame to Parquet format.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        path : str
            Destination filesystem path for the Parquet output.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the Parquet data.
        """
        df.write.mode(mode).format("parquet").save(path)

    def close_spark_session(self):
        """Closes curent active spark session"""
        self.spark.stop()

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\elasticsearch_ops.py
####################################################################################################



import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date, datetime
from typing import Any
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

from elasticsearch import AsyncElasticsearch, Elasticsearch, NotFoundError

from dlh_ai.config import get_settings
from dlh_ai.models import ChatMessage

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")
settings = get_settings()
IST = ZoneInfo("Asia/Kolkata")  # All chat session timestamps are stored in IST.


@dataclass
class ElasticsearchOps:
    """Elasticsearch helpers for FAQ retrieval and LLM response caching."""

    # FAQ index
    faq_index: str = "yono_retail_faq_document"
    faq_id_field: str = "faq_id"
    faq_version_field: str = "faq_version"
    faq_text_field: str = "combined"
    faq_vector_field: str = "embedding"

    # Cache index
    cache_index: str = "yono_retail_llm_cache_response"
    cache_text_field: str = "normalized_question"
    cache_vector_field: str = "query_embedding"

    # Chat sessions index
    chat_sessions_index: str = "yono_retail_chat_session"
    chat_session_id_field: str = "session_id"
    chat_created_at_field: str = "created_at"
    chat_updated_at_field: str = "updated_at"
    chat_messages_field: str = "message"

    @property
    def client(self) -> Elasticsearch:
        return Elasticsearch(
            hosts=settings.common.elasticsearch_hosts,
            api_key=settings.common.elasticsearch_api_key,
            verify_certs=settings.common.elasticsearch_verify_certs,
            request_timeout=settings.common.elasticsearch_request_timeout,
            max_retries=settings.common.elasticsearch_max_retries,
            retry_on_timeout=settings.common.elasticsearch_retry_on_timeout,
        )

    @property
    def aclient(self) -> AsyncElasticsearch:
        return AsyncElasticsearch(
            hosts=settings.common.elasticsearch_hosts,
            api_key=settings.common.elasticsearch_api_key,
            verify_certs=settings.common.elasticsearch_verify_certs,
            request_timeout=settings.common.elasticsearch_request_timeout,
            max_retries=settings.common.elasticsearch_max_retries,
            retry_on_timeout=settings.common.elasticsearch_retry_on_timeout,
        )

    async def hybrid_search_faq(
        self,
        index: str,
        query_text: str,
        query_embedding: list[float],
        k: int = 5,
        num_candidates: int = 50,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Hybrid search: BM25 + kNN fused with Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF)."""
        return await self._hybrid_search(
            index,
            self.faq_text_field,
            self.faq_vector_field,
            query_text,
            query_embedding,
            k,
            num_candidates,
        )

    async def text_search_cache(
        self,
        query: str,
        size: int = 10,
    ) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        """Exact cache lookup by normalized question, with FAQ version validation."""
        # Fast path: skip embedding when the normalized question matches exactly.
        result = await self._exact_text_search(
            self.cache_index, self.cache_text_field, query, size
        )
        return await self._validated_cache_hit(result)

    async def semantic_search_cache(
        self,
        query_embedding: list[float],
        k: int = 10,
        num_candidates: int = 50,
    ) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        """Semantic cache lookup by query embedding, with FAQ version validation."""
        # Fallback when exact text match misses; caller should apply a score threshold.
        result = await self._vector_search(
            self.cache_index,
            self.cache_vector_field,
            query_embedding,
            k,
            num_candidates,
        )
        return await self._validated_cache_hit(result)

    async def save_cache(
        self, document: dict[str, Any], cache_id: str
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Index a cache entry, keyed by ``cache_id``."""
        result = await self.aclient.index(
            index=self.cache_index,
            id=cache_id,
            document=document,
        )
        return result.body

    async def get_chat_session(self, session_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        """Fetch a chat session document, or ``None`` if it doesn't exist yet."""
        try:
            result = await self.aclient.get(
                index=self.chat_sessions_index, id=session_id
            )
            return result.body
        except NotFoundError:
            return None

    async def record_cache_hit(self, cache_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Atomically increment the mapped ``hit_count`` field."""
        result = await self.aclient.update(
            index=self.cache_index,
            id=cache_id,
            script={
                "source": (
                    "if (ctx._source.hit_count == null) { "
                    "if (ctx._source.cache_hit_count != null) { "
                    "ctx._source.hit_count = ctx._source.cache_hit_count; "
                    "} else { ctx._source.hit_count = 0; } }"
                    "ctx._source.hit_count += 1;"
                    "ctx._source.last_cache_hit = params.now;"
                ),
                "lang": "painless",
                "params": {"now": datetime.now(IST).isoformat()},
            },
        )
        return result.body

    async def add_chat_message(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        message: ChatMessage,
        *,
        session_date: date | None = None,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Append a message to a chat session, creating the session if needed."""
        today = session_date or datetime.now(IST).date()
        message_doc = self._serialize_chat_message(message)
        # Field names are interpolated; message payload stays in params.
        messages_field = self.chat_messages_field
        updated_at_field = self.chat_updated_at_field

        result = await self.aclient.update(
            index=self.chat_sessions_index,
            id=session_id,
            script={
                # Atomic append - ES has no native array-push API beyond scripts.
                "source": f"""
                    if (ctx._source.{messages_field} == null) {{
                        ctx._source.{messages_field} = [];
                    }}
                    ctx._source.{messages_field}.add(params.msg);
                    ctx._source.{updated_at_field} = params.updated_at;
                """,
                "lang": "painless",
                "params": {
                    "msg": message_doc,
                    "updated_at": today.isoformat(),
                },
            },
            upsert={
                self.chat_session_id_field: session_id,
                self.chat_created_at_field: today.isoformat(),
                self.chat_updated_at_field: today.isoformat(),
                # Start empty; the script appends the first message after upsert.
                self.chat_messages_field: [],
            },
            retry_on_conflict=3,  # Safe under concurrent turns in the same session.
        )
        return result.body

    def _serialize_chat_message(self, message: ChatMessage) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Map ``ChatMessage`` to the nested ``message`` object field names.

        Field names must match the nested ``message`` properties in
        ``jobs/index_creation_job.py::build_chat_sessions_index_mapping``.
        """
        return {
            "id": message.id,
            "role": message.role,
            "text": message.text,
            "retrieved_docs": [
                {"faq_id": ref.faq_id, "faq_version": ref.faq_version}
                for ref in message.retrieved_docs
            ],
            "timestamp": self._to_ist(message.timestamp).isoformat(),
            "sentiment": message.sentiment,
        }

    @staticmethod
    def _to_ist(value: datetime) -> datetime:
        # Naive datetimes are assumed to already be IST wall-clock time.
        if value.tzinfo is None:
            value = value.replace(tzinfo=IST)
        return value.astimezone(IST)

    async def _lookup_faqs_by_ids(
        self,
        index: str,
        faq_ids: list[str],
    ) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
        # Bulk-fetch current FAQ docs to compare against cached knowledge_snapshot.
        if not faq_ids:
            return {}

        unique_ids = list(dict.fromkeys(faq_ids))
        result = await self.aclient.search(
            index=index,
            size=len(unique_ids),
            query={"terms": {self.faq_id_field: unique_ids}},
        )
        faqs: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
        for hit in result.body.get("hits", {}).get("hits", []):
            source = hit.get("_source")
            if source is None:
                continue
            faq_id = source.get(self.faq_id_field)
            if faq_id is not None:
                faqs[faq_id] = source
        return faqs

    async def _is_knowledge_snapshot_valid(
        self,
        snapshot: list[dict[str, Any]],
        live_faqs: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    ) -> bool:
        # Each cached FAQ ref must still exist at the same version.
        if not snapshot:
            return True

        if live_faqs is None:
            live_faqs = await self._lookup_faqs_by_ids(
                self.faq_index, [ref["faq_id"] for ref in snapshot]
            )
        for ref in snapshot:
            live = live_faqs.get(ref["faq_id"])
            if live is None or live.get(self.faq_version_field) != ref["faq_version"]:
                logger.info(
                    "Cache snapshot mismatch: faq_id=%r cached_version=%r "
                    "live_version=%r",
                    ref.get("faq_id"),
                    ref.get("faq_version"),
                    live.get(self.faq_version_field) if live else None,
                )
                return False
        return True

    async def _validated_cache_hit(
        self, result: dict[str, Any]
    ) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        hits = result.get("hits", {}).get("hits", [])
        if not hits:
            return None

        # A query can return old duplicate cache entries. Find the first valid
        # candidate instead of rejecting the whole result because its top hit is stale.
        faq_ids = [
            ref["faq_id"]
            for hit in hits
            for ref in hit.get("_source", {}).get("knowledge_snapshot", [])
        ]
        live_faqs = await self._lookup_faqs_by_ids(self.faq_index, faq_ids)
        for hit in hits:
            source = hit.get("_source", {})
            snapshot = source.get("knowledge_snapshot", [])
            if await self._is_knowledge_snapshot_valid(snapshot, live_faqs):
                result_copy = dict(result)
                result_copy["hits"] = dict(result.get("hits", {}))
                result_copy["hits"]["hits"] = [hit]
                return result_copy

            cache_id = source.get("cache_id", hit.get("_id"))
            logger.info(
                "Cache candidate invalidated due to FAQ version mismatch "
                "(cache_id=%s)",
                cache_id,
            )
        return None

    async def _text_search(
        self,
        index: str,
        text_field: str,
        query_text: str,
        size: int,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        result = await self.aclient.search(
            index=index,
            size=size,
            query={"match": {text_field: query_text}},
        )
        return result.body

    async def _exact_text_search(
        self,
        index: str,
        text_field: str,
        query_text: str,
        size: int,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Exact full-string match via ``term`` (field should be ``keyword``)."""
        result = await self.aclient.search(
            index=index,
            size=size,
            query={"term": {text_field: {"value": query_text}}},
        )
        return result.body

    async def _vector_search(
        self,
        index: str,
        vector_field: str,
        query_embedding: list[float],
        k: int,
        num_candidates: int,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        result = await self.aclient.search(
            index=index,
            size=k,
            knn={
                "field": vector_field,
                "query_vector": query_embedding,
                "k": k,
                "num_candidates": num_candidates,
            },
        )
        return result.body

    async def _hybrid_search(
        self,
        index: str,
        text_field: str,
        vector_field: str,
        query_text: str,
        query_embedding: list[float],
        k: int,
        num_candidates: int,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        # RRF merges BM25 and kNN retriever results into a single ranked list.
        result = await self.aclient.search(
            index=index,
            size=k,
            retriever={
                "rrf": {
                    "retrievers": [
                        # Branch 1 - Text Search (BM25)
                        {
                            "standard": {
                                "query": {"match": {text_field: query_text}},
                            }
                        },
                        # Branch 2 - Vector Search (kNN)
                        {
                            "knn": {
                                "field": vector_field,
                                "query_vector": query_embedding,
                                "k": k,
                                "num_candidates": num_candidates,
                            }
                        },
                    ]
                }
            },
        )
        return result.body

from ._chat_agent import ChatAgent

    "AgentError",
    "AgentGenerationError",
    "ChatAgent",
    "build_context_items",

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\agents\_chat_agent.py
####################################################################################################



import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, cast

from langchain.agents import create_agent
from langchain.messages import AIMessage, HumanMessage, SystemMessage

from dlh_ai.genai.prompt import PromptManager
from dlh_ai.genai.rag import LlmService
from dlh_ai.genai.rag.exceptions import LlmGenerationError

from .exceptions import AgentGenerationError

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class ChatAgent:
    """Generates grounded answers for the YONO Retail chatbot.

    Renders the ``yono_retail_chatbot`` prompt templates via ``PromptManager``
    and delegates generation to an already-loaded ``LlmService`` through
    LangChain's ``create_agent`` API.

    Parameters
    ----------
    llm_service : LlmService
        A loaded (``.load()`` already called) LLM client.
    """

    llm_service: LlmService
    prompt_manager: PromptManager = field(init=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self.prompt_manager = PromptManager()

    async def answer(
        self,
        query: str,
        context_items: list[dict[str, Any]],
        history: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    ) -> str:
        """Generate an answer grounded in ``context_items`` (async).

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            The customer's question, verbatim.
        context_items : list[dict[str, Any]]
            Retrieved FAQ context, shaped by ``build_context_items``.
        history : list[dict[str, Any]] | None
            Prior turns in this session, each a ``{"query": ..., "answer": ...}``
            dict. Sent to the LLM as alternating user/assistant messages ahead
            of the current turn. Defaults to no history.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            The generated answer text.

        Raises
        ------
        AgentGenerationError
            If the LLM fails to generate an answer.
        """
        system_prompt = self.prompt_manager.get_prompt("yono_retail_chatbot", "system")
        user_prompt = self.prompt_manager.get_prompt(
            "yono_retail_chatbot", "rag_chat", query=query, context_items=context_items
        )

        messages: list = [SystemMessage(content=system_prompt)]
        for turn in history or []:
            messages.append(HumanMessage(content=turn["query"]))
            messages.append(AIMessage(content=turn["answer"]))
        messages.append(HumanMessage(content=user_prompt))

        try:
            return await self._invoke_agent(system_prompt, messages[1:])
        except LlmGenerationError:
            raise
        except Exception as exc:
            raise AgentGenerationError(
                f"Failed to generate answer for query {query!r}"
            ) from exc

    async def fallback(self, query: str) -> str:
        """Generate a polite fallback answer when no FAQ context was retrieved (async).

        Uses the ``fallback`` prompt key from ``yono_retail_chatbot.yaml``.

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            The customer's original question.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            LLM-generated fallback response.

        Raises
        ------
        AgentGenerationError
            If the LLM fails to generate the fallback.
        """
        system_prompt = self.prompt_manager.get_prompt("yono_retail_chatbot", "system")
        user_prompt = self.prompt_manager.get_prompt(
            "yono_retail_chatbot", "fallback", query=query
        )
        try:
            return await self._invoke_agent(
                system_prompt, [HumanMessage(content=user_prompt)]
            )
        except LlmGenerationError:
            raise
        except Exception as exc:
            raise AgentGenerationError(
                f"Failed to generate fallback for query {query!r}"
            ) from exc

    async def _invoke_agent(
        self, system_prompt: str, messages: list[HumanMessage | AIMessage]
    ) -> str:
        if self.llm_service._client is None:
            raise AgentGenerationError(
                "ChatAgent invoked before LlmService.load(). "
                "Call load() once during application startup."
            )

        agent = create_agent(
            model=self.llm_service._client,
            system_prompt=system_prompt,
        )
        result = await agent.ainvoke(cast(Any, {"messages": messages}))
        response = result["messages"][-1].content
        return str(response).strip()


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\agents\_context.py
####################################################################################################



from typing import Any


def build_context_items(
    hits: list[dict[str, Any]], rerank_scores: list[float] | None = None
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Convert raw Elasticsearch FAQ hits into the ``rag_chat`` prompt's shape.

    Parameters
    ----------
    hits : list[dict[str, Any]]
        Raw Elasticsearch hit objects (each with an ``_source`` and ``_score``),
        as returned by ``ElasticsearchOps.hybrid_search_faq``.
    rerank_scores : list[float] | None
        Cross-encoder scores, same order and length as ``hits``. When given,
        these replace the Elasticsearch ``_score`` for display purposes.

    Returns
    -------
    list[dict[str, Any]]
        Dicts with keys ``rank, score, category, doc_type, question, answer``,
        matching ``yono_retail_chatbot.yaml``'s ``rag_chat`` template.
    """
    items = []
    for rank, hit in enumerate(hits, start=1):
        source = hit["_source"]
        score = rerank_scores[rank - 1] if rerank_scores else hit.get("_score", 0.0)
        items.append(
            {
                "rank": rank,
                "score": score,
                "category": source["category"],
                "doc_type": source["type"],
                "question": source["question"],
                "answer": source["answer"],
            }
        )
    return items


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\agents\exceptions.py
####################################################################################################



"""
Custom exceptions for the YONO Retail chat-agent module.

All exceptions preserve the original cause via exception chaining so the
full traceback is always available without exposing secrets.
"""


class AgentError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all chat-agent errors.

    Parameters
    ----------
    message : str
        Human-readable description of the failure.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> raise AgentError("Unexpected chat-agent failure.")
    """


class AgentGenerationError(AgentError):
    """Raised when the agent fails to produce an answer from the LLM.

    Parameters
    ----------
    message : str
        The query context and underlying cause of the failure.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> raise AgentGenerationError(
    ...     "Failed to generate answer for query 'How do I reset my PIN?'"
    ... )
    """


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\prompt\__init__.py
####################################################################################################


from ._manager import PromptManager

__all__ = ["PromptManager"]


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\prompt\_manager.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import yaml
from jinja2 import Environment, StrictUndefined, Undefined

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PromptManager:
    """Manages loading and rendering prompts from YAML files in the prompt directory.

    This class provides methods to get prompts by agent name and type, and to render
    them with context variables. It supports strict template rendering and caching of
    loaded prompts.

    Attributes:
    ----------
    strict_templates : bool
        Whether to enforce strict template rendering. Defaults to True.
    
    Examples:
    --------
    >>> manager = PromptManager()
    >>> prompt = manager.get_prompt("agent_name", "instructions")
    """

    strict_templates: bool = True

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._prompt_dir = Path(__file__).parent
        self._prompt_registry = {}
        self._jinja_env = Environment(
            undefined=StrictUndefined if self.strict_templates else Undefined
        )
        self._load_all_prompts()

    def _load_all_prompts(self) -> None:
        """Eagerly load all YAML prompt files from the prompt directory."""
        self._prompt_registry.clear()

        for file_path in self._prompt_dir.glob("*.yaml"):
            agent_name = file_path.stem
            try:
                with file_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                    prompts = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}

                if not isinstance(prompts, dict):
                    logger.error(
                        f"Prompt file for agent {agent_name} must contain a YAML mapping at root. Got: {type(prompts).__name__}"
                    )
                    prompts = {}

                self._prompt_registry[agent_name] = prompts
            except Exception:
                logger.exception(
                    f"Error loading prompts for agent {agent_name} from {file_path}"
                )
                self._prompt_registry[agent_name] = {}

    def get_prompt(self, agent_name: str, prompt_key: str, **kwargs: Any) -> str:
        """Retrieves a prompt and renders it with the provided keyword arguments.

        Parameters:
        -----------
           agent_name (str): The name of the agent. This must match the filename of the YAML file in the prompt directory.
           prompt_key (str): The key of the prompt within the agent's YAML file.
           **kwargs (Any): Keyword arguments to pass to the Jinja2 template which will replace variable with actual value during runtime.
           
        Returns:
        --------
           str: The rendered prompt string.

        Raises:
           ValueError: If the prompt is not found or the template rendering fails due missing template variables.

        Example:
        --------
            >>> pm = PromptManager()
            >>> prompt = pm.get_prompt("agent1", "system", variable="value")
            >>> prompt2 = pm.get_prompt("my_awesome_agent", "instruction", column_name=["col1", "col2", "col3"])

        Notes:
        ------
            - The `agent_name` and `prompt_key` are used to locate the prompt in the registry.
            - The `**kwargs` are used to render the Jinja2 template.
        """
        prompts = self._prompt_registry.get(agent_name, {})
        template_str = prompts.get(prompt_key, "")
        if not isinstance(template_str, str) or not template_str:
            raise ValueError(f"Prompt not found: {agent_name}.{prompt_key}")

        template = self._jinja_env.from_string(template_str)
        return template.render(**kwargs)

    def clear_cache(self, agent_name: str | None = None) -> None:
        if agent_name is None:
            self._prompt_registry.clear()
        else:
            self._prompt_registry.pop(agent_name, None)

    def reload(self) -> None:
        """Reload all prompt YAML files from disk."""
        self._load_all_prompts()
