"""Heuristic input and output checks for the YONO Retail RAG chatbot.

Input checks run before retrieval; output checks run after generation. The
module uses regular expressions, word lists, and token-overlap scores only.
It does not load models, call an LLM, or make network requests.
"""

import logging
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from better_profanity import profanity

from ._models import ChatRequest, ChatResponse, SearchHit
from ._preprocessing import _BANKING_KEYWORDS, preprocess_query
from .exceptions import (
    HallucinationDetectedError,
    InvalidCitationError,
    OutOfDomainError,
    PIIDetectedError,
    ProfanityDetectedError,
    PromptInjectionDetectedError,
)

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")



_PII_PATTERNS: dict[str, re.Pattern[str]] = {
    # Indian PAN: 5 uppercase letters, 4 digits, 1 uppercase letter.
    "IN_PAN": re.compile(r"\b[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]\b"),
    # Aadhaar: 12 digits, optionally grouped in blocks of 4 with space or dash.
    "IN_AADHAAR": re.compile(r"\b\d{4}[-\s]?\d{4}[-\s]?\d{4}\b"),
    # Credit/debit card: 13-19 digits, optionally split by spaces or dashes.
    "CREDIT_CARD": re.compile(r"\b(?:\d[ -]?){13,19}\b"),
    # Indian mobile: optional +91 prefix, then a digit 6-9 followed by 9 more digits.
    "PHONE_NUMBER": re.compile(r"\b(?:\+91[-\s]?)?[6-9]\d{9}\b"),
    # Standard e-mail address.
    "EMAIL_ADDRESS": re.compile(r"\b[\w.+-]+@[\w-]+\.[\w.-]+\b"),
    # IBAN: 2-letter country code, 2 check digits, 10-30 alphanumeric characters.
    "IBAN_CODE": re.compile(r"\b[A-Z]{2}\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{10,30}\b"),
    # Generic Indian bank account number heuristic: 9-18 consecutive digits.
    # Intentionally broad - overlaps with card/Aadhaar but the overlap resolver
    # below keeps only the longest (most specific) match.
    "ACCOUNT_NUMBER": re.compile(r"\b\d{9,18}\b"),
}

_DEFAULT_PII_ENTITIES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "CREDIT_CARD",
    "IN_PAN",
    "IN_AADHAAR",
    "PHONE_NUMBER",
    "EMAIL_ADDRESS",
    "IBAN_CODE",
    "ACCOUNT_NUMBER",
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _PiiMatch:
    entity_type: str
    start: int
    end: int
    text: str


def _detect_pii(text: str, entities: list[str]) -> list[_PiiMatch]:
    """Find requested PII patterns and return non-overlapping matches."""
    candidates: list[_PiiMatch] = []
    for entity_type in entities:
        pattern = _PII_PATTERNS.get(entity_type)
        if pattern is None:
            continue
        for m in pattern.finditer(text):
            candidates.append(_PiiMatch(entity_type, m.start(), m.end(), m.group()))

    # Longest match first; ties broken by the caller-supplied priority order.
    priority = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(entities)}
    candidates.sort(
        key=lambda m: (-len(m.text), priority.get(m.entity_type, len(entities)))
    )

    accepted: list[_PiiMatch] = []
    covered: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
    for m in candidates:
        # Reject this candidate if its span overlaps any already-accepted span.
        if any(m.start < e and m.end > s for s, e in covered):
            continue
        accepted.append(m)
        covered.append((m.start, m.end))

    # Put back in left-to-right order so _redact_pii can walk the string once.
    accepted.sort(key=lambda m: m.start)
    return accepted


def _redact_pii(text: str, matches: list[_PiiMatch]) -> str:
    """Replace each matched PII span with its typed placeholder."""
    if not matches:
        return text
    parts: list[str] = []
    last = 0
    for m in matches:
        parts.append(text[last : m.start])  # text before this PII span
        parts.append(f"<{m.entity_type}>")  # the placeholder label
        last = m.end  # skip past the matched span
    parts.append(text[last:])  # remainder after last match
    return "".join(parts)



_INJECTION_PATTERNS: tuple[re.Pattern[str], ...] = tuple(
    re.compile(pattern, re.IGNORECASE)
    for pattern in (
        r"ignore\s+(?:all\s+)?(?:previous|prior|above)\s+instructions",
        r"disregard\s+(?:all\s+)?(?:previous|prior|above)\s+instructions",
        r"pretend\s+(?:you\s+are|to\s+be)",
        r"you\s+are\s+now\s+(?:a|an)\b",
        r"forget\s+(?:you\s+are|your\s+instructions)",
        r"reveal\s+your\s+(?:system\s+)?(?:prompt|instructions|configuration)",
        r"override\s+your\s+instructions",
        r"\bjailbreak\b",
        r"\bDAN\b",
        r"\[\s*system\s*\]",
    )
)



_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[a-zA-Z]+")


def _tokenize(text: str) -> set[str]:
    """Return lowercase alphabetic tokens from ``text``."""
    return {w.lower() for w in _WORD_RE.findall(text)}



_STOPWORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"the", "a", "an", "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being", "to", "of", "and", "or", "in", "on", "at", "for", "with", "as", "by", "this", "that", "it", "you", "your", "i", "we", "can", "will", "please", "from", "if", "not", "no", "do", "does", "did", "have", "has", "had", "my", "me", "us", "our"})


def _content_tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    """Return meaningful lowercase tokens with stopwords removed."""
    return {t for t in _tokenize(text) if t not in _STOPWORDS and len(t) > 2}


def _grounding_score(answer: str, context_text: str) -> float:
    """Return the fraction of answer tokens found in the retrieved context."""
    answer_tokens = _content_tokens(answer)
    if not answer_tokens:
        # Nothing meaningful to check - pass through.
        return 1.0
    context_tokens = _content_tokens(context_text)
    return len(answer_tokens & context_tokens) / len(answer_tokens)


_FINANCE_ADVICE_KEYWORDS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "invest",
    "recommend",
    "should buy",
    "financial advice",
    "portfolio",
    "returns",
)


@dataclass
class _OutputState:
    """Tiny carrier object threaded through the output-validation pipeline.

    Rather than passing ``(response, context_text)`` as two separate
    arguments to every output-check method, we bundle them here so each
    check can read or update ``state.response`` and then pass the whole
    object on to the next step.
    """

    response: ChatResponse
    context_text: str


class GuardrailService:
    """Validate chat input and generated answers using lightweight checks.

    Notes
    -----
    Input checks cover preprocessing, profanity, PII, prompt injection, and
    banking-topic validation. Output checks cover grounding, PII redaction,
    citation scores, and financial-advice disclaimers.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        pii_entities: list[str] | None = None,
        enable_preprocessing: bool = True,
        enable_pii_redaction: bool = True,
        enable_profanity_filter: bool = True,
        grounding_threshold: float = 0.15,
        banking_keywords: frozenset[str] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        """Initialise the guardrail checks and their thresholds.

        Parameters
        ----------
        pii_entities : list[str] or None
            PII pattern names to enable. Uses the default set when omitted.
        enable_preprocessing : bool
            Whether to normalise and validate the query before other checks.
        enable_pii_redaction : bool
            Whether detected PII should be replaced instead of rejected.
        enable_profanity_filter : bool
            Whether to apply the profanity word-list check.
        grounding_threshold : float
            Minimum answer-to-context token overlap.
        banking_keywords : frozenset[str] or None
            Domain terms used by the topic check.
        """
        # better-profanity loads its built-in word list into memory once here.
        # This is the only "setup" cost - no model weights, no file downloads.
        logger.info("GuardrailService: loading better-profanity word list")
        profanity.load_censor_words()

        self.enable_preprocessing: bool = enable_preprocessing
        self.enable_profanity_filter: bool = enable_profanity_filter
        self.grounding_threshold: float = grounding_threshold
        self.pii_entities: list[str] = pii_entities or list(_DEFAULT_PII_ENTITIES)
        self.enable_pii_redaction: bool = enable_pii_redaction
        self.banking_keywords: frozenset[str] = banking_keywords or _BANKING_KEYWORDS

        logger.info("GuardrailService initialised (heuristic-only, no model load)")

    # Public API

    def validate_input(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Validate and, when enabled, redact a customer query.

        Parameters
        ----------
        request : ChatRequest
            Incoming customer request.

        Returns
        -------
        ChatRequest
            The validated request.

        Raises
        ------
        GuardrailViolationError
            If a language, profanity, PII, injection, or topic check fails.
        """
        request = self._preprocess(request)
        request = self._check_profanity(request)
        request = self._detect_and_handle_pii(request)
        request = self._check_prompt_injection(request)
        request = self._check_banking_topic(request)
        return request

    def validate_output(
        self,
        response: ChatResponse,
        retrieved_context: list[SearchHit],
    ) -> ChatResponse:
        """Validate a generated answer against context and citation scores.

        Parameters
        ----------
        response : ChatResponse
            Generated answer before final delivery.
        retrieved_context : list[SearchHit]
            FAQ passages used to ground the answer.

        Returns
        -------
        ChatResponse
            Validated response, possibly with redaction or a disclaimer.
        """
        # Join all retrieved passages into one big string so the grounding
        # and PII checks have a single text surface to work against.
        context_text = "\n".join(hit.answer for hit in retrieved_context)
        state = _OutputState(response=response, context_text=context_text)
        state = self._check_grounding(state)
        state = self._redact_output_pii(state)
        state = self._check_citations(state)
        state = self._apply_disclaimer(state)
        return state.response

    # Input checks

    def _preprocess(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Apply query preprocessing when the feature is enabled."""
        # Skip this step entirely when the feature flag is off - the raw
        # query is passed straight through to the profanity check.
        if not self.enable_preprocessing:
            return request
        return preprocess_query(request)

    def _check_profanity(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Reject profane queries when the filter is enabled."""
        # Skip this check entirely when the feature flag is off.
        if not self.enable_profanity_filter:
            return request
        # better-profanity does a simple word-list scan - O(n) in query length.
        if profanity.contains_profanity(request.query):
            logger.warning("Profanity detected in user query")
            raise ProfanityDetectedError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails: profane language detected in query"
            )
        return request

    def _detect_and_handle_pii(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Redact detected PII or reject it when redaction is disabled."""
        # Run all enabled PII regex patterns against the raw query.
        matches = _detect_pii(request.query, self.pii_entities)
        if not matches:
            # Nothing found - return the request unchanged.
            return request

        entity_types = [m.entity_type for m in matches]
        logger.warning("PII detected in user query (types: %s)", entity_types)

        if self.enable_pii_redaction:
            # Replace every matched span with a "<ENTITY_TYPE>" placeholder
            # and hand the sanitised query downstream.  The original string
            # is never modified - model_copy returns a new Pydantic instance.
            redacted = _redact_pii(request.query, matches)
            logger.info("PII redacted from user query")
            return request.model_copy(update={"query": redacted})

        # Redaction is off but PII was found - block the request outright.
        raise PIIDetectedError(f"PII detected in query: {entity_types}")

    def _check_prompt_injection(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Reject queries matching known prompt-injection patterns."""
        # Try each jailbreak pattern against the query; stop on first match.
        # re.search checks anywhere in the string (not just the start).
        if any(pattern.search(request.query) for pattern in _INJECTION_PATTERNS):
            logger.warning(
                "Prompt-injection attempt detected in query: '%s'",
                request.query[:80],
            )
            raise PromptInjectionDetectedError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails (possible prompt injection or "
                "role-override attempt)"
            )
        return request

    def _check_banking_topic(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Reject non-empty queries with no banking-related terms."""
        # Tokenize the query into lowercase words then intersect with the
        # banking keyword set.  If there is zero overlap AND the query is
        # non-empty, it is almost certainly off-topic.
        tokens = _tokenize(request.query)
        if tokens and not (tokens & self.banking_keywords):
            logger.warning(
                "Out-of-domain query blocked by banking-topic guardrail: '%s'",
                request.query[:80],
            )
            raise OutOfDomainError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails (out-of-domain request, please "
                "ask a YONO banking-related question)"
            )
        return request

    # Output checks

    def _check_grounding(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        """Reject answers whose token overlap is below the configured threshold."""
        # Compute the lexical overlap ratio between the answer and the
        # joined context passages, then reject if it falls below the
        # configured threshold.
        score = _grounding_score(state.response.answer, state.context_text)
        if score < self.grounding_threshold:
            logger.error(
                "Hallucination detected: grounding score %.4f below threshold %.4f",
                score,
                self.grounding_threshold,
            )
            raise HallucinationDetectedError(
                "Generated answer contains claims not grounded in retrieved "
                f"context (grounding score {score:.4f} below threshold "
                f"{self.grounding_threshold:.2f})"
            )
        return state

    def _redact_output_pii(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        """Redact sensitive identifiers found in the generated answer."""
        # Run the same PII regex pipeline on the LLM's answer text.
        # The LLM occasionally echoes back numbers or identifiers from the
        # query even after input redaction, so we scrub the output too.
        answer = state.response.answer
        matches = _detect_pii(answer, self.pii_entities)
        if not matches:
            return state
        logger.warning(
            "PII detected in LLM output (types: %s); redacting.",
            [m.entity_type for m in matches],
        )
        redacted = _redact_pii(answer, matches)
        # model_copy returns a new ChatResponse - the original is never mutated.
        state.response = state.response.model_copy(update={"answer": redacted})
        return state

    def _check_citations(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        """Reject responses that cite sources with non-positive scores."""
        # The reranker assigns a relevance score to each retrieved passage.
        # A score of 0 or below means the reranker considered the passage
        # irrelevant to the query - if the LLM cited it anyway, that is a
        # citation integrity problem and we refuse to return the answer.
        zero_score_sources = [hit for hit in state.response.sources if hit.score <= 0]
        if zero_score_sources:
            logger.error(
                "Citation validation failed: %d cited source(s) have a "
                "non-positive relevance score (categories: %s)",
                len(zero_score_sources),
                [hit.category for hit in zero_score_sources],
            )
            raise InvalidCitationError(
                "Generated answer cites source(s) with no genuine relevance "
                "to the query"
            )
        return state

    def _apply_disclaimer(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        """Append a disclaimer when the answer resembles financial advice."""
        # Simple substring scan - no regex needed because the trigger phrases
        # are short and well-defined.  If any match, append the regulatory
        # disclaimer to the answer via model_copy (immutable update).
        answer = state.response.answer
        if any(kw in answer.lower() for kw in _FINANCE_ADVICE_KEYWORDS):
            answer += (
                "\n\n**Disclaimer**: This is general information only. "
                "For personalised financial advice, please consult a licensed advisor."
            )
            state.response = state.response.model_copy(update={"answer": answer})
        return state


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_llm_service.py
####################################################################################################



"""
LLM client for the YONO Retail chatbot - wraps any OpenAI-compatible
chat-completions endpoint (config default: a self-hosted vLLM server)
via LangChain's ``ChatOpenAI``, with a persistent httpx connection pool.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import httpx
from langchain_core.messages import BaseMessage
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

from .exceptions import LlmGenerationError, LlmLoadError

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

_DLH_EMPTY_KEY: str = "EMPTY"

_str_parser = StrOutputParser()


@dataclass
class LlmService:
    """OpenAI-compatible chat-completion client. Call ``load()`` once at startup, then ``generate()`` / ``agenerate()`` per request."""

    model_name: str
    base_url: str
    api_key: str
    verify_ssl: bool = False
    temperature: float = 0.2
    max_tokens: int = 3000
    request_timeout: float = 60.0
    connection_pool_size: int = 100
    max_retries: int = 3

    _client: ChatOpenAI | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _http_client: httpx.Client | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _async_http_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)

    # Public lifecycle ---------------------------------------------------

    def load(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the OpenAI-compatible client with connection pooling.

        Must be called once before any :meth:`generate` or :meth:`agenerate` call.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If ``base_url`` is empty or the ``ChatOpenAI`` constructor fails.
        """
        self._load_dlh_client()

    def generate(self, messages: list[BaseMessage]) -> str:
        """Send a conversation to the LLM and return the generated text (sync).

        Transient errors (connection failures, HTTP 429/5xx) are retried
        internally by the underlying OpenAI client up to ``max_retries``
        times with exponential backoff.

        Parameters
        ----------
        messages : list[BaseMessage]
            Full conversation to send, e.g.
            ``[SystemMessage(...), HumanMessage(...), AIMessage(...), ...]``.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            Generated answer text, stripped of leading/trailing whitespace.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If :meth:`load` has not been called.
        LlmGenerationError
            If the LLM endpoint fails after all retries.
        """
        if self._client is None:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                "LlmService.generate() called before LlmService.load(). "
                "Call load() once during application startup."
            )

        logger.debug(
            "Sending completion request to model '%s' (base_url=%s).",
            self.model_name,
            self.base_url,
        )
        try:
            response = self._client.invoke(messages)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise LlmGenerationError(
                f"LLM generation failed for model '{self.model_name}' at "
                f"'{self.base_url}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc

        answer = str(response.content).strip()
        logger.debug("LLM generation complete. Response length: %d chars.", len(answer))
        return answer

    async def agenerate(self, messages: list[BaseMessage]) -> str:
        """Send a conversation to the LLM and return the generated text (async).

        Uses ``ChatOpenAI.ainvoke`` so no thread-pool wrapping is needed in
        async request handlers.  The LCEL ``| StrOutputParser()`` step is
        applied inline so the return type is always a plain ``str``.

        Parameters
        ----------
        messages : list[BaseMessage]
            Full conversation to send.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            Generated answer text, stripped of leading/trailing whitespace.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If :meth:`load` has not been called.
        LlmGenerationError
            If the LLM endpoint fails after all retries.
        """
        if self._client is None:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                "LlmService.agenerate() called before LlmService.load(). "
                "Call load() once during application startup."
            )

        logger.debug(
            "Sending async completion request to model '%s' (base_url=%s).",
            self.model_name,
            self.base_url,
        )
        try:
            # LCEL mini-chain: llm | StrOutputParser  (no extra class needed)
            chain = self._client | _str_parser
            answer = await chain.ainvoke(messages)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise LlmGenerationError(
                f"LLM async generation failed for model '{self.model_name}' at "
                f"'{self.base_url}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc

        answer = answer.strip()
        logger.debug("LLM async generation complete. Response length: %d chars.", len(answer))
        return answer

    async def shutdown(self) -> None:
        """Close the underlying sync and async HTTP connection pools.

        Call this from the FastAPI lifespan shutdown handler.
        """
        if self._http_client is not None:
            try:
                self._http_client.close()
                logger.info("LLM sync HTTP connection pool closed.")
            except OSError as exc:
                logger.warning("Failed to close LLM sync HTTP client: %s", exc)

        if self._async_http_client is not None:
            try:
                await self._async_http_client.aclose()
                logger.info("LLM async HTTP connection pool closed.")
            except OSError as exc:
                logger.warning("Failed to close LLM async HTTP client: %s", exc)

    # Private loader -------------------------------------------------------

    def _load_dlh_client(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the OpenAI-compatible client with a pooled httpx transport.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If ``base_url`` is empty or the ``ChatOpenAI`` constructor fails.
        """
        if not self.base_url:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                "DLH base URL is empty. "
                "Set 'LLM__BASE_URL' in the environment or config.toml."
            )

        if not self.verify_ssl:
            logger.warning(
                "LLM client: TLS certificate verification is DISABLED. "
                "This must only be used in UAT with self-signed certificates, "
                "set LLM__VERIFY_SSL=true in production."
            )

        # Persistent httpx.Client with an explicit connection pool.
        # max_connections controls keep-alive pool size; limits concurrent
        # requests to prevent overwhelming the LLM server.
        self._http_client = httpx.Client(
            transport=httpx.HTTPTransport(verify=self.verify_ssl, retries=0),
            verify=self.verify_ssl,
            limits=httpx.Limits(
                max_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                max_keepalive_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                keepalive_expiry=30.0,
            ),
            headers={"Connection": "keep-alive"},
        )

        # Mirrors _http_client above but async - without this, ChatOpenAI
        # falls back to the OpenAI SDK's own default AsyncClient for
        # agenerate(), which ignores verify_ssl and the pool settings here.
        self._async_http_client = httpx.AsyncClient(
            transport=httpx.AsyncHTTPTransport(verify=self.verify_ssl, retries=0),
            verify=self.verify_ssl,
            limits=httpx.Limits(
                max_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                max_keepalive_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                keepalive_expiry=30.0,
            ),
            headers={"Connection": "keep-alive"},
        )

        try:
            self._client = ChatOpenAI(
                model=self.model_name,
                api_key=self.api_key or _DLH_EMPTY_KEY,
                base_url=self.base_url,
                timeout=self.request_timeout,
                max_retries=self.max_retries,
                temperature=self.temperature,
                max_tokens=self.max_tokens,
                http_client=self._http_client,
                http_async_client=self._async_http_client,
            )
            logger.info(
                "DLH LLM client initialised, model: '%s' | base_url: '%s' | "
                "pool_size: %d | retries: %d",
                self.model_name,
                self.base_url,
                self.connection_pool_size,
                self.max_retries,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                f"Failed to initialise DLH OpenAI-compatible client "
                f"at '{self.base_url}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_models.py
####################################################################################################



"""
Pydantic models / data-transfer objects for the active RAG pipeline.

Only the three models actually used by the live pipeline are kept here:

* ``SearchHit``    - one reranked FAQ result passed to GuardrailService.
* ``ChatRequest``  - guardrail input wrapper (query text only).
* ``ChatResponse`` - guardrail output wrapper (answer + sources).
"""

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field


class SearchHit(BaseModel):
    """A single ranked FAQ result used by ``GuardrailService``.

    Attributes
    ----------
    score : float
        Reranker or Elasticsearch relevance score.
    sno : int
        Sequential row number from the knowledge-base CSV (set to 0 when
        constructed from a live ES hit that has no ``sno`` field).
    category : str
        YONO service category (e.g. "ATM/Debit Card Service").
    question : str
        The FAQ question text.
    answer : str
        The FAQ answer text.
    doc_type : str
        Document classification label (e.g. "Main Category").
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    score: float
    sno: int
    category: str
    question: str
    answer: str
    doc_type: str


class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    """Input wrapper passed through the guardrail input pipeline.

    Only ``query`` is used by ``GuardrailService``; the model is kept
    minimal so guardrail checks remain independent of the API request shape.

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        The customer's natural-language question, verbatim.
    """

    query: str = Field(..., min_length=1, max_length=2000)


class ChatResponse(BaseModel):
    """Output wrapper passed through the guardrail output pipeline.

    Built from the LLM-generated answer and the reranked search hits
    in ``_to_guardrail_response()`` inside ``chat_pipeline.py``.

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        Echo of the original user query.
    answer : str
        LLM-generated answer, possibly modified by guardrail checks
        (PII redaction, disclaimer appended).
    category : str
        YONO category of the top-ranked source hit.
    doc_type : str
        Document type of the top-ranked source hit.
    score : float
        Relevance score of the top-ranked source hit.
    sources : list[SearchHit]
        All reranked hits used to ground the answer.
    """

    query: str
    answer: str
    category: str
    doc_type: str
    score: float
    sources: list[SearchHit] = Field(default_factory=list)


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_preprocessing.py
####################################################################################################



"""
Query preprocessing for the YONO Retail RAG chatbot.

Runs as step 0 of ``GuardrailService.validate_input``, before profanity/
PII/injection/topic - a short, ambiguous, non-English, or acronym-heavy
query fails those later checks (or retrieval) for reasons that have
nothing to do with the query's actual content. Everything here is a
regex match, dict lookup, or Unicode range check: no ML models, no
network calls.
"""

import logging
import re
import unicodedata

from ._models import ChatRequest
from .exceptions import ClarificationNeededError, UnsupportedLanguageError

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


# could drift apart.
_BANKING_KEYWORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "yono", "sbi", "bank", "banking", "account", "accounts", "savings",
        "current", "balance", "deposit", "deposits", "withdraw", "withdrawal",
        "transfer", "neft", "rtgs", "imps", "upi", "ifsc", "branch", "atm",
        "debit", "credit", "card", "cards", "cvv", "pin", "otp", "kyc",
        "loan", "loans", "emi", "interest", "fd", "rd", "ppf", "nominee",
        "nomination", "netbanking", "internet", "mobile", "app", "passbook",
        "statement", "statements", "cheque", "chequebook", "draft", "swift",
        "iban", "aadhaar", "pan", "insurance", "premium", "mutual", "fund",
        "funds", "investment", "tax", "gst", "remittance", "forex", "locker",
        "overdraft", "minimum", "complaint", "grievance", "activate",
        "block", "unblock", "transaction", "transactions", "payee",
        "beneficiary", "wallet", "merchant", "qr", "pos", "recharge", "bill",
        "payment", "payments", "pay", "limit", "charges", "charge", "fee",
        "fees", "customer", "care", "helpline", "service", "services",
        "request", "application", "apply", "renew", "renewal", "reset",
        "update", "register", "registration", "login", "password", "mpin",
        "biometric",
    }
)

_ACRONYM_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    "fd": "fixed deposit", "rd": "recurring deposit",
    "ppf": "public provident fund", "sb": "savings bank account",
    "ca": "current account", "mab": "monthly average balance",
    "neft": "national electronic funds transfer",
    "rtgs": "real time gross settlement",
    "imps": "immediate payment service", "upi": "unified payments interface",
    "nach": "national automated clearing house",
    "ecs": "electronic clearing service",
    "kyc": "know your customer", "aml": "anti money laundering",
    "otp": "one time password", "mpin": "mobile pin",
    "cvv": "card verification value", "ifsc": "indian financial system code",
    "pan": "permanent account number",
    "emi": "equated monthly instalment", "gst": "goods and services tax",
    "dd": "demand draft", "od": "overdraft",
}

_SYNONYM_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    "forgot password": "forgot password reset credentials",
    "reset password": "reset password credentials",
    "change password": "change password credentials",
    "password not working": "password issue reset credentials",
    "cannot login": "login issue password credentials",
    "can't login": "login issue password credentials",
    "unable to login": "login issue password credentials",
    "login problem": "login issue password credentials",
    "authorise": "authorise authorize authenticate",
    "authorize": "authorise authorize authenticate",
    "authentication": "authorise authenticate",
}

_CLARIFICATION_TRIGGERS: list[tuple[str, str | None]] = [
    (
        (
            r"\b(forgot|forget|reset|change|lost)\s+(my\s+)?"
            r"(password|credentials|login|mpin|pin|id|user\s*id|username)\b"
        ),
        (
            "Which would you like to reset - your YONO Retail login password, "
            "MPIN, or ATM/Debit card PIN?"
        ),
    ),
    (
        r"\b(not\s+working|not\s+loading|error|issue|problem|trouble|failing)\b",
        (
            "Could you tell me which feature or transaction is not working? "
            "For example - login, fund transfer, card, or FD/RD?"
        ),
    ),
    (
        (
            r"^\s*(hi|hello|hey|good\s+morning|good\s+afternoon|good\s+evening|"
            r"namaste)\s*\.?\s*$"
        ),
        None,
    ),
    (
        r"\b(help|assist|support)\s*\??\s*$",
        (
            "Of course! Could you tell me what you need help with? For example "
            "- account services, cards, loans, or FD/RD?"
        ),
    ),
    (r"^\s*\w{1,3}\s*$",
     "Could you provide a bit more detail about what you'd like to know?"),
]

_CLARIFICATION_EXCEPTIONS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    r"\b(how|what|when|where|why|steps|process|procedure)\b",
    r"\b(enable|disable|activate|deactivate)\b",
    r"\b(limit|charge|fee|rate)\b",
)

_SHORT_QUERY_WORD_THRESHOLD = 10

_DOMAIN_SIGNAL_WORDS: tuple[str, ...] = ("yono", "sbi", "retail")

_NON_ENGLISH_RANGES: tuple[tuple[int, int, str], ...] = (
    (0x0900, 0x097F, "Hindi (Devanagari)"), (0x0980, 0x09FF, "Bengali"),
    (0x0A00, 0x0A7F, "Punjabi (Gurmukhi)"), (0x0A80, 0x0AFF, "Gujarati"),
    (0x0B00, 0x0B7F, "Odia"), (0x0B80, 0x0BFF, "Tamil"),
    (0x0C00, 0x0C7F, "Telugu"), (0x0C80, 0x0CFF, "Kannada"),
    (0x0D00, 0x0D7F, "Malayalam"), (0x0600, 0x06FF, "Arabic/Urdu"),
    (0x4E00, 0x9FFF, "Chinese"), (0x3040, 0x309F, "Japanese Hiragana"),
    (0xAC00, 0xD7AF, "Korean"),
)

_NON_ENGLISH_MESSAGE = (
    "I currently support queries only in English. Support for {language} "
    "will be added soon. Please type your question in English and I will "
    "be happy to assist you."
)


def _detect_language(text: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the name of the first non-English script found in *text*, if any."""
    for char in text:
        cp = ord(char)
        for start, end, lang in _NON_ENGLISH_RANGES:
            if start <= cp <= end:
                return lang
    return None


def _normalise_special_chars(text: str) -> str:
    """Fold Unicode dash/quote variants and stray "word*word" asterisks to a consistent form."""
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)
    text = re.sub(r"[–-−‐‑‒]", "-", text)
    text = re.sub(r"(?<=[A-Za-z])\*(?=[A-Za-z])", "-", text)
    text = text.replace("‘", "'").replace("’", "'")
    text = text.replace("“", '"').replace("”", '"')
    text = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text)
    return text.strip()


def _check_needs_clarification(text: str) -> str | None:
    """Return a clarification question if *text* is too ambiguous to act on, else None."""
    text_lower = text.lower().strip()
    if any(
        re.search(pattern, text_lower, re.IGNORECASE)
        for pattern in _CLARIFICATION_EXCEPTIONS
    ):
        return None
    for pattern, question in _CLARIFICATION_TRIGGERS:
        if re.search(pattern, text_lower, re.IGNORECASE):
            return question
    return None


def _expand_acronyms(text: str) -> str:
    """Expand whole-word banking acronyms (longest key first) unless already spelled out."""
    result = text
    for acronym in sorted(_ACRONYM_MAP, key=len, reverse=True):
        expansion = _ACRONYM_MAP[acronym]
        if expansion.lower() in result.lower():
            continue
        pattern = rf"\b{re.escape(acronym)}\b"
        if re.search(pattern, result, re.IGNORECASE):
            result = re.sub(pattern, expansion, result, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    return result


def _expand_synonyms(text: str) -> str:
    """Rewrite common troubleshooting phrases (longest key first) onto a shared vocabulary."""
    result = text
    for phrase in sorted(_SYNONYM_MAP, key=len, reverse=True):
        pattern = re.escape(phrase)
        if re.search(pattern, result, re.IGNORECASE):
            result = re.sub(pattern, _SYNONYM_MAP[phrase], result, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    return result


def _add_implicit_domain_context(text: str) -> str:
    """Ready-to-enable hook for prepending domain context to short, bare queries.

    Currently a no-op - every branch below falls through to returning
    *text* unchanged.  Kept fully wired (word-count / banking-noun /
    domain-signal checks) so flipping the final line on is a one-line
    change if implicit-context injection turns out to be wanted.
    """
    words = text.lower().split()
    if len(words) >= _SHORT_QUERY_WORD_THRESHOLD:
        return text
    if not any(w in _BANKING_KEYWORDS for w in words):
        return text
    if any(signal in text.lower() for signal in _DOMAIN_SIGNAL_WORDS):
        return text
    # return f"{text} in YONO Retail"
    return text


def preprocess_query(request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
    """Normalise, validate, and expand ``request.query`` before any other guardrail runs.

    Parameters
    ----------
    request : ChatRequest
        Incoming chat request, not yet touched by any guardrail.

    Returns
    -------
    ChatRequest
        *request* unchanged if nothing needed rewriting, otherwise a copy
        with ``query`` normalised/expanded.

    Raises
    ------
    UnsupportedLanguageError
        The query is written in a non-English script.
    ClarificationNeededError
        The query is too short or ambiguous to act on directly.
    """
    # Non-English input can't be answered against an English-only FAQ
    # base - block it before spending a retrieval + LLM call on it.
    language = _detect_language(request.query)
    if language:
        logger.info("Non-English query blocked (detected: %s)", language)
        raise UnsupportedLanguageError(
            _NON_ENGLISH_MESSAGE.format(language=language)
        )

    text = _normalise_special_chars(request.query)

    # Checked before acronym/synonym expansion: a bare "FD" should still
    # read as too short to act on, rather than being padded out by
    # expansion before the ambiguity check ever sees it.
    clarification = _check_needs_clarification(text)
    if clarification:
        logger.info("Query needs clarification: '%s'", request.query[:80])
        raise ClarificationNeededError(clarification)

    text = _expand_acronyms(text)
    text = _expand_synonyms(text)
    text = _add_implicit_domain_context(text)

    if text == request.query:
        return request
    logger.info("Query preprocessed: '%s' -> '%s'", request.query[:80], text[:80])
    return request.model_copy(update={"query": text})


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\exceptions.py
####################################################################################################



"""
Custom exceptions for the YONO Retail RAG chatbot pipeline.

Only the exceptions that are actually raised or caught by live code are
kept here. Each class maps to a specific failure scenario so callers can
handle them individually or catch the whole hierarchy with ``YonoRagError``.
"""


# Base


class YonoRagError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all YONO RAG pipeline errors."""


# Configuration


class RagConfigurationError(YonoRagError):
    """Raised when the RAG pipeline configuration is invalid or incomplete."""


# LLM provider


class LlmLoadError(YonoRagError):
    """Raised when the LLM client cannot be initialised.

    Raised by ``LlmService._load_dlh_client`` when ``base_url`` is empty
    or the ``OpenAI`` constructor fails.
    """


class LlmGenerationError(YonoRagError):
    """Raised when the LLM endpoint fails to generate a completion.

    Raised by ``LlmService.generate`` after all retries are exhausted,
    caught and re-raised by ``ChatAgent`` as ``AgentGenerationError``.
    """


# Guardrails


class GuardrailViolationError(YonoRagError):
    """Base for all guardrail violations.

    Caught by ``InputGuardrailHandler`` and ``OutputGuardrailHandler``
    in ``chat_pipeline.py`` to set ``ctx.blocked_reason``.
    """


class PIIDetectedError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when PII is detected and ``enable_pii_redaction`` is ``False``.

    When redaction is enabled (default) PII is silently replaced instead.
    """


class ProfanityDetectedError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when a user query contains profane language."""


class ToxicContentError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Reserved for future toxicity-filter implementations.

    Not currently raised by any live code - ``GuardrailService`` uses
    only heuristic/regex checks and does not run an ML toxicity model.
    Kept in the exception hierarchy so external callers that catch it
    do not break if a toxicity check is re-introduced later.
    """


class PromptInjectionDetectedError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when a prompt-injection / role-override pattern is detected."""


class OutOfDomainError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when the query has no banking-domain vocabulary."""


class HallucinationDetectedError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when the answer's grounding score falls below ``grounding_threshold``."""


class InvalidCitationError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when a cited source has a non-positive relevance score."""


class UnsupportedLanguageError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when the query is written in a non-English script."""


class ClarificationNeededError(GuardrailViolationError):
    """Raised when the query is too short or ambiguous to act on directly."""

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\sentiment\_lexicon.py
####################################################################################################



"""
Hand-rolled VADER-style lexicon sentiment scorer.

No external NLP dependency (no ``nltk``/``vaderSentiment``) — word-level
polarity plus negation flips, intensifier boosts, and a bounded compound-score
normalisation is a well-known, small enough algorithm to reimplement directly
for short banking-chat messages.
"""

import math
import re

_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[a-zA-Z']+")

_LEXICON: dict[str, float] = {
    # Positive
    "good": 1.9, "great": 3.1, "excellent": 3.3, "amazing": 3.4, "awesome": 3.2,
    "helpful": 2.4, "happy": 2.7, "pleased": 2.3, "satisfied": 2.2, "thanks": 2.0,
    "thank": 2.0, "appreciate": 2.2, "wonderful": 3.1, "perfect": 3.0,
    "love": 3.0, "easy": 1.7, "fast": 1.4, "quick": 1.4, "resolved": 2.1,
    "convenient": 1.8, "nice": 1.8, "smooth": 1.6, "best": 3.2, "like": 1.5,
    "glad": 2.1, "impressed": 2.4, "efficient": 1.9, "clear": 1.2, "works": 1.1,
    "working": 1.1, "smoothly": 1.6,
    # Negative
    "bad": -2.5, "terrible": -3.4, "horrible": -3.4, "awful": -3.1, "worst": -3.4,
    "poor": -2.0, "useless": -2.8, "frustrated": -2.6, "frustrating": -2.6,
    "angry": -2.7, "annoyed": -2.2, "annoying": -2.2, "disappointed": -2.3,
    "disappointing": -2.3, "problem": -1.7, "issue": -1.4, "error": -1.8,
    "fail": -2.2, "failed": -2.2, "failure": -2.3, "broken": -2.2, "blocked": -1.8,
    "fraud": -3.0, "fraudulent": -3.0, "scam": -2.9, "unauthorized": -2.4,
    "delay": -1.6, "delayed": -1.6, "slow": -1.5, "stuck": -1.8, "confusing": -1.7,
    "confused": -1.5, "wrong": -1.8, "unable": -1.6, "difficult": -1.6,
    "hard": -1.2, "worried": -1.9, "concerned": -1.5, "urgent": -1.3, "complaint": -2.0,
    "unhappy": -2.4, "rude": -2.4, "waste": -2.0, "hate": -3.0,
}

_NEGATIONS = {"not", "no", "never", "cannot", "without", "hardly", "barely", "isn't", "doesn't"}
_INTENSIFIERS: dict[str, float] = {
    "very": 0.9, "extremely": 1.3, "really": 0.8, "so": 0.6, "totally": 1.0,
    "absolutely": 1.2, "quite": 0.4, "somewhat": -0.3, "slightly": -0.5,
}

_NEGATION_LOOKBACK = 3  # words
_NEGATION_SCALAR = -0.74  # VADER-style dampened negation flip (not a hard -1x)
_ALPHA = 15.0  # VADER's compound-score normalisation constant


def _tokenize(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [w.lower() for w in _WORD_RE.findall(text)]


def _is_negated(window: list[str]) -> bool:
    return any(w in _NEGATIONS or w.endswith("n't") for w in window)


def _is_polite_request_like(tokens: list[str], i: int) -> bool:
    """True if the "like" at ``tokens[i]`` is the "would like to"/"'d like to"
    request idiom, not the sentiment verb (e.g. "I would like to check my
    balance" vs. "I really like this app")."""
    prev_token = tokens[i - 1] if i > 0 else ""
    next_token = tokens[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(tokens) else ""
    return next_token == "to" and (prev_token == "would" or prev_token.endswith("'d"))


def compute_polarity(text: str) -> float:
    """Compute a compound polarity score for ``text``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    text : str
        Input text (typically a single chat message).

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Compound polarity score in ``[-1.0, 1.0]``. Positive values indicate
        positive sentiment, negative values indicate negative sentiment, and
        values near zero indicate neutral sentiment or no lexicon matches.
    """
    tokens = _tokenize(text)
    if not tokens:
        return 0.0

    total = 0.0
    for i, token in enumerate(tokens):
        if token == "like" and _is_polite_request_like(tokens, i):
            continue

        base = _LEXICON.get(token)
        if base is None:
            continue

        score = base
        window = tokens[max(0, i - _NEGATION_LOOKBACK) : i]
        for w in window:
            if w in _INTENSIFIERS:
                score += math.copysign(_INTENSIFIERS[w], score)
        if _is_negated(window):
            score *= _NEGATION_SCALAR

        total += score

    # VADER-style squash into (-1, 1) without a hard clip.
    return total / math.sqrt(total * total + _ALPHA)


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\sentiment\_service.py
####################################################################################################



from dataclasses import dataclass

from ._lexicon import compute_polarity


@dataclass
class SentimentResult:
    """Result of a sentiment analysis call.

    Attributes
    ----------
    label : str
        One of ``"positive"``, ``"neutral"``, ``"negative"``.
    score : float
        Compound polarity score in ``[-1.0, 1.0]``.
    """

    label: str
    score: float


@dataclass
class SentimentService:
    """Lexicon-based (VADER-style) sentiment scorer for chat messages.

    Parameters
    ----------
    positive_threshold : float
        Minimum compound score classified as "positive". Default ``0.05``
        (VADER's own convention).
    negative_threshold : float
        Maximum compound score classified as "negative". Default ``-0.05``.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> SentimentService().analyze("This was extremely helpful, thank you!")
    SentimentResult(label='positive', score=...)
    """

    positive_threshold: float = 0.05
    negative_threshold: float = -0.05

    def analyze(self, text: str) -> SentimentResult:
        """Score ``text`` and classify it as positive, neutral, or negative."""
        score = compute_polarity(text)
        if score >= self.positive_threshold:
            label = "positive"
        elif score <= self.negative_threshold:
            label = "negative"
        else:
            label = "neutral"
        return SentimentResult(label=label, score=score)

import logging
import pickle
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import cmlapi
import mlflow
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


def load_mlflow_model_from_registry(
    project_id: str, model_id: str
) -> mlflow.pyfunc.PyFuncModel:
    """Fetch and load the MLflow model from the CML AI registry.

    Parameters
    ----------
    project_id : str
        The ID of the project in the AI registry.
    model_id : str
        The ID of the registered model to retrieve.

    Returns
    -------
    mlflow.pyfunc.PyFuncModel
        The loaded MLflow model ready for inference.
    """
    client = cmlapi.default_client()
    # getting model from AI registry
    registered_model = client.get_registered_model(model_id).to_dict()
    logger.debug(f"fetched model details of {registered_model['name']}")

    # getting model path from model metadata and loading the model using mlflow
    exp = registered_model["model_versions"][0]["model_version_metadata"][
        "mlflow_metadata"
    ]["experiment_id"]
    run = registered_model["model_versions"][0]["model_version_metadata"][
        "mlflow_metadata"
    ]["run_id"]
    model_run = client.get_experiment_run(
        project_id=project_id, experiment_id=exp, run_id=run
    ).to_dict()

    # NOTE - /home/cdsw has to be appended as thats correct path & not /.experiemnt that comes in metadata
    return mlflow.pyfunc.load_model(
        f"/home/cdsw{model_run['data']['registered_model_metadata'][0]['model_path']}"
    )


def load_mlflow_model_from_registry_as_spark(
    spark: SparkSession, project_id: str, model_id: str
):
    """Fetch and load the MLflow model from the CML AI registry.

    Parameters
    ----------
    project_id : str
        The ID of the project in the AI registry.
    model_id : str
        The ID of the registered model to retrieve.

    Returns
    -------
    mlflow.pyfunc.PyFuncModel
        The loaded MLflow model ready for inference.
    """
    client = cmlapi.default_client()
    # getting model from AI registry
    registered_model = client.get_registered_model(model_id).to_dict()
    logger.debug(f"fetched model details of {registered_model['name']}")

    # getting model path from model metadata and loading the model using mlflow
    exp = registered_model["model_versions"][0]["model_version_metadata"][
        "mlflow_metadata"
    ]["experiment_id"]
    run = registered_model["model_versions"][0]["model_version_metadata"][
        "mlflow_metadata"
    ]["run_id"]
    model_run = client.get_experiment_run(
        project_id=project_id, experiment_id=exp, run_id=run
    ).to_dict()

    # NOTE - /home/cdsw has to be appended as thats correct path & not /.experiemnt that comes in metadata
    return mlflow.pyfunc.spark_udf(
        spark,
        model_uri=f"/home/cdsw{model_run['data']['registered_model_metadata'][0]['model_path']}",
    )


def load_model_pickle_from_local(self, path: str | Path):
    """
    Load a trained model pickle from disk.

    Args:
        path: Path to the saved model

    Returns:
        model weights
    """
    path = Path(path)
    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Model file not found: {path}")

    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        logger.info(f"loading model from {path}")
        return pickle.load(f)


class ModelInference:
    """Class for performing inference with trained models."""

    model: Any

    def predict(
        self,
        X: np.ndarray | pd.DataFrame,
        return_proba: bool = False,
    ) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Make predictions on input data.

        Args:
            X: Input features (numpy array or pandas DataFrame)
            return_proba: If True, return probability estimates (for classifiers)

        Returns:
            Predictions as numpy array
        """

        if isinstance(X, pd.DataFrame):
            X = X.values

        if return_proba and hasattr(self.model, "predict_proba"):
            return self.model.predict_proba(X)

        return self.model.predict(X)

    def predict_single(
        self,
        features: list[float] | dict[str, float],
        feature_names: list[str] | None = None,
        return_proba: bool = False,
    ) -> float | int | np.ndarray:
        """
        Make prediction for a single sample.

        Args:
            features: Feature values as list or dictionary
            feature_names: Optional list of feature names (required if features is dict)
            return_proba: If True, return probability estimates

        Returns:
            Single prediction value or probability array
        """
        if isinstance(features, dict):
            if feature_names is None:
                feature_names = list(features.keys())
            features = [features[name] for name in feature_names]

        X = np.array(features).reshape(1, -1)
        prediction = self.predict(X, return_proba=return_proba)
        logger.info("successful prediction")

        return prediction[0]

    def batch_predict(
        self,
        X: np.ndarray | pd.DataFrame,
        return_proba: bool = False,
        batch_size: int = 1000,
    ) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Make predictions in batches for large datasets.

        Args:
            X: Input features
            batch_size: Number of samples per batch
            return_proba: If True, return probability estimates

        Returns:
            Predictions as numpy array
        """

        if isinstance(X, pd.DataFrame):
            X = X.values

        n_samples = X.shape[0]
        predictions = []

        for idx, i in enumerate(range(0, n_samples, batch_size), start=1):
            batch = X[i : i + batch_size]
            batch_pred = self.predict(batch, return_proba=return_proba)
            predictions.append(batch_pred)
            logger.debug(
                f"batch: {idx} out of: {i}/{n_samples // batch_size} processed"
            )
        logger.info("successfully generated predictions for all batches")

        return np.concatenate(predictions)

    def predict_with_confidence(
        self,
        X: np.ndarray | pd.DataFrame,
        confidence_threshold: float = 0.5,
    ) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
        """
        Make predictions with confidence scores (for classifiers).

        Args:
            X: Input features
            confidence_threshold: Minimum confidence threshold

        Returns:
            Tuple of (predictions, confidence_scores, high_confidence_mask)
        """

        probabilities = self.predict(X, return_proba=True)
        predictions = np.argmax(probabilities, axis=1)
        confidence_scores = np.max(probabilities, axis=1)
        high_confidence_mask = confidence_scores >= confidence_threshold

        logger.debug("successfully generated confidence scores")
        return predictions, confidence_scores, high_confidence_mask

    def explain_prediction(
        self,
        features: list[float] | dict[str, float],
        feature_names: list[str] | None = None,
    ) -> dict:
        """
        Provide explanation for a single prediction (basic version).

        Args:
            features: Feature values
            feature_names: Optional list of feature names

        Returns:
            Dictionary with prediction and basic explanation
        """
        prediction = self.predict_single(features, feature_names)
        logger.info("successfully generated prediction")

        explanation = {
            "prediction": float(prediction)
            if isinstance(prediction, (np.integer, np.floating))
            else prediction.tolist(),
            "features": features
            if isinstance(features, dict)
            else dict(zip(feature_names or [], features)),
        }
        logger.info("successfully generated explanation")

        # Add feature importance if available
        if hasattr(self.model, "feature_importances_"):
            if feature_names:
                importance = dict(zip(feature_names, self.model.feature_importances_))
                explanation["feature_importance"] = importance
                logger.info("successfully generated feature importance")
        else:
            logger.warning("model does not provide feature importance")

        return explanation

FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\training.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

import pandas as pd
from sklearn.metrics import classification_report, confusion_matrix
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split
from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class ModelTrainer:
    """Base class for training machine learning models.

    Parameters
    ----------
    data : pd.DataFrame
        The complete dataset containing features and target column.
    target_column : str
        Name of the column containing the target variable.
    test_size : float, optional
        Proportion of dataset to include in test split, by default 0.2.
    random_state : int, optional
        Random seed for reproducibility, by default 47.

    Examples
    --------

    >>> trainer = ModelTrainer(
    ...     data=data,
    ...     target_column='target',
    ...     test_size=0.3,
    ...     random_state=42
    ... )

    >>> # Train model (sample usage)
    >>> model = trainer.train_decision_tree({'max_depth': 5, 'min_samples_split': 10})
    >>>
    >>> # Get evaluation metrics
    >>> metrics = trainer.evaluation_metrics_report(model)
    """

    data: pd.DataFrame
    target_column: str
    test_size: float = 0.2
    random_state: int = 47

    def __post_init__(self):
        X = self.data.drop(columns=[self.target_column], axis=1)
        y = self.data[self.target_column]

        self.x_train, self.x_test, self.y_train, self.y_test = train_test_split(
            X, y, test_size=self.test_size, random_state=self.random_state
        )

    def train_decision_tree(self, model_params: dict) -> DecisionTreeClassifier:
        """Train a Decision Tree classifier model.

        Trains a scikit-learn DecisionTreeClassifier using the training data
        split during initialization.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model_params : dict
            Dictionary of parameters to pass to DecisionTreeClassifier.
            Common parameters include:
            - max_depth : int, maximum depth of the tree
            - min_samples_split : int, minimum samples required to split
            - min_samples_leaf : int, minimum samples required at leaf node
            - criterion : str, function to measure split quality ('gini', 'entropy')

        Returns
        -------
        DecisionTreeClassifier
            Trained decision tree model.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> import pandas as pd
        >>> from sklearn.datasets import load_iris
        >>>
        >>> # Load sample data
        >>> iris = load_iris()
        >>> data = pd.DataFrame(iris.data, columns=iris.feature_names)
        >>> data['target'] = iris.target
        >>>
        >>> # Initialize trainer
        >>> trainer = ModelTrainer(data=data, target_column='target')
        >>>
        >>> # Train with custom parameters
        >>> model = trainer.train_decision_tree({
        ...     'max_depth': 3,
        ...     'min_samples_split': 5,
        ...     'criterion': 'gini',
        ...     'random_state': 42
        ... })
        >>>
        >>> # Make predictions
        >>> predictions = model.predict(trainer.x_test)

        See Also
        --------
        evaluation_metrics_report : Get comprehensive evaluation metrics
        """
        logger.info("training decision tree classifier")
        model = DecisionTreeClassifier(**model_params)
        model.fit(self.x_train, self.y_train)
        logger.info("decision tree classifier model training completed")
        return model

    def evaluation_metrics_report(self, model):
        """Generate comprehensive evaluation metrics for a trained model.

        Calculates various classification metrics for both training and testing
        datasets, including accuracy, specificity, sensitivity, confusion matrices,
        and detailed classification reports.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model : sklearn classifier
            A trained scikit-learn like classifier with predict and predict_proba methods.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            Dictionary containing the following keys:
            - classification_report_train : str
                Detailed classification report for training data
            - classification_report_test : str
                Detailed classification report for testing data
            - confusion_matrix_train : numpy.ndarray
                Confusion matrix for training data
            - confusion_matrix_test : numpy.ndarray
                Confusion matrix for testing data
            - accuracy_train : float
                Accuracy score on training data
            - specificity_train : float
                Specificity (true negative rate) on training data
            - sensitivity_train : float
                Sensitivity (recall/true positive rate) on training data
            - accuracy_test : float
                Accuracy score on testing data
            - specificity_test : float
                Specificity (true negative rate) on testing data
            - sensitivity_test : float
                Sensitivity (recall/true positive rate) on testing data

        Examples
        --------
        >>> import pandas as pd
        >>> from sklearn.datasets import make_classification
        >>>
        >>> # Create sample data
        >>> X, y = make_classification(n_samples=500, n_features=10, random_state=42)
        >>> data = pd.DataFrame(X, columns=[f'feature_{i}' for i in range(10)])
        >>> data['target'] = y
        >>>
        >>> # Train model
        >>> trainer = ModelTrainer(data=data, target_column='target')
        >>> model = trainer.train_decision_tree({'max_depth': 5})
        >>>
        >>> # Get evaluation metrics
        >>> metrics = trainer.evaluation_metrics_report(model)
        >>>
        >>> # Access specific metrics
        >>> print(f"Test Accuracy: {metrics['accuracy_test']:.3f}")
        Test Accuracy: 0.850
        >>> print(f"Test Sensitivity: {metrics['sensitivity_test']:.3f}")
        Test Sensitivity: 0.820
        >>> print(f"Test Specificity: {metrics['specificity_test']:.3f}")
        Test Specificity: 0.880
        >>>
        >>> # View confusion matrix
        >>> print("Confusion Matrix (Test):")
        >>> print(metrics['confusion_matrix_test'])
        Confusion Matrix (Test):
        [[44  6]
         [ 9 41]]

        Notes
        -----
        - Specificity measures the proportion of actual negatives correctly identified
        - Sensitivity (recall) measures the proportion of actual positives correctly identified
        - The confusion matrix threshold is set to 0.5 by default

        See Also
        --------
        train_decision_tree : Train a decision tree classifier
        """
        cm_train = self._confusion_matrix_report(self.x_train, self.y_train, model)
        cm_test = self._confusion_matrix_report(self.x_test, self.y_test, model)
        total_train = sum(sum(cm_train))
        total_test = sum(sum(cm_test))

        accuracy_train = (cm_train[0, 0] + cm_train[1, 1]) / total_train
        specificity_train = cm_train[0, 0] / (cm_train[0, 0] + cm_train[0, 1])
        sensitivity_train = cm_train[1, 1] / (cm_train[1, 0] + cm_train[1, 1])

        accuracy_test = (cm_test[0, 0] + cm_test[1, 1]) / total_test
        specificity_test = cm_test[0, 0] / (cm_test[0, 0] + cm_test[0, 1])
        sensitivity_test = cm_test[1, 1] / (cm_test[1, 0] + cm_test[1, 1])

        report = {
            "classification_report_train": self._classification_report(
                self.x_train, self.y_train, model
            ),
            "classification_report_test": self._classification_report(
                self.x_test, self.y_test, model
            ),
            "confusion_matrix_train": cm_train,
            "confusion_matrix_test": cm_test,
            "accuracy_train": accuracy_train,
            "specificity_train": specificity_train,
            "sensitivity_train": sensitivity_train,
            "accuracy_test": accuracy_test,
            "specificity_test": specificity_test,
            "sensitivity_test": sensitivity_test,
        }
        return report

    @staticmethod
    def _classification_report(x, y, model):
        return classification_report(y, model.predict(x))

    @staticmethod
    def _confusion_matrix_report(x, y, model, threshold=0.5):
        return confusion_matrix(
            y, (model.predict_proba(x)[:, 1] >= threshold).astype(int)
        )


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\__init__.py
####################################################################################################


"""
This is the single import entry point for the entire drift monitoring library.
Every public class, schema, and convenience wrapper is re-exported here so that
downstream scripts, pytest suites, MLflow runs, and Airflow DAGs never need to
know which sub-module a symbol lives in.

Package layout (flat — all modules sit at the repo root)
---------------------------------------------------------
    statistical_tests.py    KS, Chi-Square, Anderson-Darling, Mann-Whitney U
    distribution_metrics.py Jensen-Shannon, KL, Wasserstein, Hellinger, Total Variation
    stability_metrics.py    PSI, CSI, DSI
    performance_drift.py    Accuracy / Precision / Recall / F1 / AUC drift + degradation
    prediction_drift.py     Prediction distribution, class imbalance, confidence, error, bias
    feature_drift.py        Per-feature, multivariate (MMD/energy), correlation, importance
    drift_detector.py       Orchestrator — wires all six specialist detectors
    schemas.py              Pydantic v2 models for the four database tables


How to use this package in a drift or ML test script
-----------------------------------------------------
Import the orchestrator for a single-call full-scan::

    from drift import DriftDetector

    detector = DriftDetector(threshold=0.05)
    report   = detector.detect_all_drifts(reference_data, current_data)
    print(detector.generate_report(report))

Import specialist classes for targeted checks::

    from drift import StatisticalTests, StabilityMetrics

    ks  = StatisticalTests(threshold=0.05).kolmogorov_smirnov_test(ref, curr)
    psi = StabilityMetrics(bins=10).population_stability_index(ref, curr)

Import convenience wrappers for one-liner usage (no class instantiation)::

    from drift import (
        kolmogorov_smirnov_test,
        population_stability_index,
        jensen_shannon_divergence,
        feature_drift_detector,
        accuracy_drift,
    )

    result = kolmogorov_smirnov_test(ref_array, curr_array, threshold=0.05)
    # result["drift_detected"], result["drift_severity"], result["statistic"]

Import Pydantic schemas for type-safe data ingestion / serialisation::

    from drift import PredictionRecord, DriftResultRecord

    record = PredictionRecord(
        model_name="fraud_detector",
        model_id="v1.0.0",
        prediction_timestamp=datetime.utcnow(),
        prediction=0.87,
        prediction_label="fraud",
    )
    print(record.model_dump_json())


Class reference summary
-----------------------
DriftDetector
    Orchestrator. Wraps all six specialist detectors. Accepts a ``threshold``
    and per-category enable/disable flags. Call ``detect_all_drifts`` for a full
    scan, or individual ``detect_feature_drift`` / ``detect_prediction_drift`` /
    ``detect_performance_drift`` for targeted checks.

    Use in drift pipelines, Airflow tasks, or CI regression tests.

StatisticalTests
    Four classical hypothesis tests that detect distributional shift between two
    1-D numpy arrays (reference vs current):
        kolmogorov_smirnov_test  — max CDF distance; best for continuous distributions
        chi_square_test          — histogram goodness-of-fit; good for binned/categorical
        anderson_darling_test    — tail-sensitive; more power than KS in the tails
        mann_whitney_test        — non-parametric rank test; works on non-normal data

    All methods return p_value, statistic, drift_detected, and drift_severity.

DistributionMetrics
    Five information-theoretic / geometric distance measures computed on shared
    histograms:
        jensen_shannon_divergence  — symmetric, bounded [0, 1]; JS > 0.1 = drift
        kullback_leibler_divergence — asymmetric KL(Q||P); KL > 0.1 = drift
        wasserstein_distance       — Earth Mover's Distance, std-normalised
        hellinger_distance         — Euclidean distance between √PMFs; bounded [0, 1]
        total_variation_distance   — half L1 distance between PMFs; bounded [0, 1]

    Use when you need a signed or bounded distance measure rather than a p-value.

StabilityMetrics
    Three industry-standard stability metrics used in banking / credit-risk:
        population_stability_index  — PSI; < 0.1 none, 0.1–0.2 moderate, > 0.2 retrain
        characteristic_stability_index — CSI; max relative bin deviation; tail-sensitive
        data_stability_index        — DSI; composite of mean/variance/skew/kurtosis shifts

    Preferred when reporting to risk teams or when PSI thresholds are mandated.

FeatureDrift
    Four methods for feature-level drift analysis:
        feature_drift_detector   — KS / Chi-Square / Mann-Whitney over every feature key
                                   in a dict[str, np.ndarray]; returns drifted_features list
        multivariate_drift       — MMD (RBF kernel) or Energy Distance on 2-D matrices;
                                   detects joint / correlated shifts invisible per-feature
        correlation_drift        — Pearson correlation structure change; flags changed pairs
        feature_importance_drift — Importance value change + Spearman rank correlation

    Use in feature monitoring pipelines and model explanation workflows.

PredictionDrift
    Five methods covering every aspect of prediction output drift:
        prediction_distribution_shift — KS on raw scores; captures mean/std shift
        class_imbalance_change        — per-class proportion change (classification)
        prediction_confidence_drift   — mean confidence change + KS on probabilities
        prediction_error_drift        — MAE/RMSE change for regression residuals
        prediction_bias_drift         — systematic signed-error change (over/under-predict)

    Use after model deployment to monitor what the model is outputting.

PerformanceDrift
    Seven methods for detecting metric regression between evaluation windows:
        accuracy_drift              — |Δaccuracy| vs threshold
        precision_drift             — |Δprecision| vs threshold
        recall_drift                — |Δrecall| vs threshold
        f1_drift                    — |ΔF1| vs threshold
        auc_drift                   — |ΔAUC-ROC| vs threshold
        performance_degradation_score — weighted composite; only negative changes count
        metric_stability_check      — rolling CV + trend over a history list

    Use to gate model promotion, trigger retraining alerts, or feed SLO dashboards.

PredictionRecord
    Pydantic v2 model for the ``predictions`` table. One row per inference call.
    Contains model identifiers, input features (dict), raw prediction, class label,
    per-class probabilities, ground-truth feedback, latency, and data quality flags.

MetricRecord
    Pydantic v2 model for the ``metrics`` table. One row per evaluation window.
    Covers all classification metrics (accuracy, precision, recall, F1, AUC, log-loss,
    confusion matrix counts) and all regression metrics (MSE, RMSE, MAE, R², MAPE).

DriftResultRecord
    Pydantic v2 model for the ``drift_results`` table. One row per detection run.
    Stores the overall drift flag, severity, per-test numeric results, drifted feature
    names, performance deltas, reference/current time windows, and alert information.

StatisticsRecord
    Pydantic v2 model for the ``statistics`` table. One row per feature snapshot.
    Contains count, mean, std, percentiles (p25/p50/p75/p95/p99), skewness, kurtosis,
    missing-value counts, mode, histogram bins/counts, correlation with target, and
    outlier summary.

"""


# Orchestrator

from .drift_detector import DriftDetector



from .statistical_tests import (StatisticalTests, _validate_arrays)

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\distribution_metrics.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Distribution distance metrics for drift detection.

Provides Jensen-Shannon divergence, Kullback-Leibler divergence, Wasserstein
distance, Hellinger distance, and Total Variation distance.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
from scipy.spatial.distance import jensenshannon

from .statistical_tests import _validate_arrays

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

DriftResult = dict[str, float | bool | str]


@dataclass
class DistributionMetrics:
    """
    Distribution distance metrics for drift detection.

    All computation logic lives inside this class. Instantiate once with a
    default bin count and call metric methods directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bins : int, optional
        Default number of histogram bins used when no per-call value is
        supplied. Must be >= 2. Default is ``10``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``bins < 2``.
    """

    _EPSILON: ClassVar[float] = 1e-10

    bins: int = field(default=10)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.bins < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {self.bins}.")

    def _normalize(self, hist: np.ndarray, bins: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Smooth and normalise a histogram to a probability vector.

        Parameters
        ----------
        hist : np.ndarray
            Raw bin counts.
        bins : int
            Number of bins (used for epsilon scaling).

        Returns
        -------
        np.ndarray
            Smoothed probability vector summing to approximately 1.
        """
        return (hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON) / (
            hist.sum() + bins * self._EPSILON
        )

    def _shared_bins(self, ref: np.ndarray, curr: np.ndarray, bins: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Compute shared histogram bin edges from the union of both samples.

        Parameters
        ----------
        ref : np.ndarray
            Reference 1-D array.
        curr : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D array.
        bins : int
            Number of bins.

        Returns
        -------
        np.ndarray
            Array of ``bins + 1`` bin edge values.
        """
        return np.histogram_bin_edges(np.concatenate([ref, curr]), bins=bins)

    @staticmethod
    def _severity(value: float, low: float, medium: float, high: float) -> str:
        """
        Map a scalar metric value to a severity label.

        Parameters
        ----------
        value : float
            The metric value to classify.
        low : float
            Upper bound of the ``"low"`` range.
        medium : float
            Upper bound of the ``"medium"`` range.
        high : float
            Upper bound of the ``"high"`` range.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            One of ``"low"``, ``"medium"``, ``"high"``, or ``"critical"``.
        """
        if value < low:
            return "low"
        if value < medium:
            return "medium"
        if value < high:
            return "high"
        return "critical"

    def jensen_shannon_divergence(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Jensen-Shannon (JS) divergence between two distributions.

        Symmetric and bounded in [0, 1]. A value of 0 means identical
        distributions; 1 means maximally different.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Jensen-Shannon Divergence"``.
            ``js_divergence`` : float
                JS divergence in [0, 1].
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``js_divergence > 0.1``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``bins < 2``.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "jensen_shannon_divergence")
        if b < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {b}.")
        logger.debug("Computing Jensen-Shannon divergence with %d bins.", b)
        edges = self._shared_bins(reference_data, current_data, b)
        ref_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        curr_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        js_div = float(jensenshannon(ref_prob, curr_prob) ** 2)
        return {
            "metric_name": "Jensen-Shannon Divergence",
            "js_divergence": js_div,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(js_div, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3),
            "drift_detected": bool(js_div > 0.1),
        }

    def kullback_leibler_divergence(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence from reference to current.

        Asymmetric relative-entropy measure. Unbounded; large values indicate
        that the current distribution is very different from the reference.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array (the ``P`` distribution).
        current_data : np.ndarray
