            Current 1-D sample array (the ``Q`` distribution).
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Kullback-Leibler Divergence"``.
            ``kl_divergence`` : float
                KL divergence KL(Q || P). Values > 0.1 indicate drift.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``kl_divergence > 0.1``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``bins < 2``.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "kullback_leibler_divergence")
        if b < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {b}.")
        logger.debug("Computing KL divergence with %d bins.", b)
        edges = self._shared_bins(reference_data, current_data, b)
        ref_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        curr_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        kl_div = float(np.sum(curr_prob * np.log(curr_prob / ref_prob)))
        return {
            "metric_name": "Kullback-Leibler Divergence",
            "kl_divergence": kl_div,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(kl_div, 0.1, 0.5, 1.0),
            "drift_detected": bool(kl_div > 0.1),
        }

    def wasserstein_distance(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Wasserstein-1 distance (Earth Mover's Distance), normalised by reference std.

        Measures the minimum cost of transforming one distribution into the
        other. The raw distance is normalised by the reference standard
        deviation so results are comparable across features of different scales.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Wasserstein Distance"``.
            ``wasserstein_distance`` : float
                Raw Wasserstein-1 distance.
            ``normalized_distance`` : float
                Distance divided by reference standard deviation.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``
                (based on normalised distance; thresholds 0.1 / 0.3 / 0.5).
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``normalized_distance > 0.1``.
        """
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "wasserstein_distance")
        logger.debug("Computing Wasserstein distance.")
        distance = float(stats.wasserstein_distance(reference_data, current_data))
        normalized = distance / (float(np.std(reference_data)) + self._EPSILON)
        return {
            "metric_name": "Wasserstein Distance",
            "wasserstein_distance": distance,
            "normalized_distance": normalized,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(normalized, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5),
            "drift_detected": bool(normalized > 0.1),
        }

    def hellinger_distance(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Hellinger distance between two distributions.

        Symmetric and bounded in [0, 1]. Equivalent to the Euclidean distance
        between the square roots of the probability vectors.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Hellinger Distance"``.
            ``hellinger_distance`` : float
                Hellinger distance in [0, 1].
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``hellinger_distance > 0.1``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``bins < 2``.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "hellinger_distance")
        if b < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {b}.")
        logger.debug("Computing Hellinger distance with %d bins.", b)
        edges = self._shared_bins(reference_data, current_data, b)
        ref_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        curr_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        hellinger = float(
            np.sqrt(np.sum((np.sqrt(ref_prob) - np.sqrt(curr_prob)) ** 2)) / np.sqrt(2)
        )
        return {
            "metric_name": "Hellinger Distance",
            "hellinger_distance": hellinger,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(hellinger, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3),
            "drift_detected": bool(hellinger > 0.1),
        }

    def total_variation_distance(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Total Variation (TV) distance between two distributions.

        Bounded in [0, 1]. Equals half the L1 distance between the probability
        mass functions, representing the maximum possible probability difference
        between the two distributions over any event.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Total Variation Distance"``.
            ``tv_distance`` : float
                TV distance in [0, 1].
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``tv_distance > 0.1``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``bins < 2``.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "total_variation_distance")
        if b < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {b}.")
        logger.debug("Computing Total Variation distance with %d bins.", b)
        edges = self._shared_bins(reference_data, current_data, b)
        ref_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        curr_prob = self._normalize(np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)[0], b)
        tv_distance = float(0.5 * np.sum(np.abs(ref_prob - curr_prob)))
        return {
            "metric_name": "Total Variation Distance",
            "tv_distance": tv_distance,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(tv_distance, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3),
            "drift_detected": bool(tv_distance > 0.1),
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\drift_detector.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Comprehensive drift detector orchestrating all drift detection methods.

Provides a unified interface for feature drift, prediction drift, performance drift,
and univariate statistical drift, aggregating results into a single severity verdict.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np

from .distribution_metrics import DistributionMetrics
from .feature_drift import FeatureDrift
from .performance_drift import PerformanceDrift
from .prediction_drift import PredictionDrift
from .stability_metrics import StabilityMetrics
from .statistical_tests import StatisticalTests

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

_SEVERITY_RANK: dict[str, int] = {
    "none": 0,
    "low": 1,
    "medium": 2,
    "high": 3,
    "critical": 4,
}
_RANK_SEVERITY: dict[int, str] = {v: k for k, v in _SEVERITY_RANK.items()}

DriftReport = dict[str, object]
MetricsDict = dict[str, float]
FeatureDict = dict[str, np.ndarray]


@dataclass
class DriftDetector:
    """
    Comprehensive drift detector combining multiple detection strategies.

    Internally instantiates ``StatisticalTests``, ``DistributionMetrics``,
    ``StabilityMetrics``, ``FeatureDrift``, ``PredictionDrift``, and
    ``PerformanceDrift``, then calls their methods directly. Each detection
    category can be independently enabled or disabled via constructor flags.

    Parameters
    ----------
    threshold : float, optional
        Default p-value significance level for all statistical tests. Must
        satisfy ``0 < threshold < 1``. Default is ``0.05``.
    enable_statistical : bool, optional
        Run KS, Chi-Square, Anderson-Darling, and Mann-Whitney U tests.
        Default is ``True``.
    enable_distribution : bool, optional
        Run JS, KL, Wasserstein, Hellinger, and TV distance metrics.
        Default is ``True``.
    enable_stability : bool, optional
        Run PSI, CSI, and DSI stability metrics. Default is ``True``.
    enable_performance : bool, optional
        Run performance metric drift checks. Default is ``True``.
    enable_feature : bool, optional
        Run per-feature and multivariate drift detection. Default is ``True``.
    enable_prediction : bool, optional
        Run prediction distribution drift checks. Default is ``True``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``threshold`` is not in the open interval (0, 1).
    """

    threshold: float = field(default=0.05)
    enable_statistical: bool = field(default=True)
    enable_distribution: bool = field(default=True)
    enable_stability: bool = field(default=True)
    enable_performance: bool = field(default=True)
    enable_feature: bool = field(default=True)
    enable_prediction: bool = field(default=True)

    # Specialist sub-detectors — initialised in __post_init__
    _stat_tests: StatisticalTests = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _dist_metrics: DistributionMetrics = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _stability: StabilityMetrics = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _feat_drift: FeatureDrift = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _pred_drift: PredictionDrift = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _perf_drift: PerformanceDrift = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 0.0 < self.threshold < 1.0:
            raise ValueError(f"threshold must be in (0, 1), got {self.threshold}.")

        self._stat_tests = StatisticalTests(threshold=self.threshold)
        self._dist_metrics = DistributionMetrics()
        self._stability = StabilityMetrics()
        self._feat_drift = FeatureDrift(threshold=self.threshold)
        self._pred_drift = PredictionDrift(threshold=self.threshold)
        self._perf_drift = PerformanceDrift(threshold=self.threshold)

        logger.debug(
            "DriftDetector initialized (threshold=%.3f, statistical=%s, distribution=%s, "
            "stability=%s, performance=%s, feature=%s, prediction=%s).",
            self.threshold,
            self.enable_statistical,
            self.enable_distribution,
            self.enable_stability,
            self.enable_performance,
            self.enable_feature,
            self.enable_prediction,
        )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public detection methods
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    def detect_data_drift(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
    ) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Detect drift in a single univariate array using all enabled methods.

        Runs the full suite of enabled statistical tests, distribution metrics,
        and stability metrics on a single pair of 1-D arrays.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any enabled method reports drift.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all enabled methods.
            ``methods`` : dict[str, dict]
                Per-method result dicts keyed by method name.
        """
        methods: dict[str, DriftReport] = {}

        if self.enable_statistical:
            methods["ks_test"] = self._stat_tests.kolmogorov_smirnov_test(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["chi_square"] = self._stat_tests.chi_square_test(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["anderson_darling"] = self._stat_tests.anderson_darling_test(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["mann_whitney"] = self._stat_tests.mann_whitney_test(
                reference_data, current_data
            )

        if self.enable_distribution:
            methods["js_divergence"] = self._dist_metrics.jensen_shannon_divergence(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["kl_divergence"] = self._dist_metrics.kullback_leibler_divergence(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["wasserstein"] = self._dist_metrics.wasserstein_distance(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["hellinger"] = self._dist_metrics.hellinger_distance(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["tv_distance"] = self._dist_metrics.total_variation_distance(
                reference_data, current_data
            )

        if self.enable_stability:
            methods["psi"] = self._stability.population_stability_index(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["csi"] = self._stability.characteristic_stability_index(
                reference_data, current_data
            )
            methods["dsi"] = self._stability.data_stability_index(
                reference_data, current_data
            )

        return {
            "drift_detected": any(
                m.get("drift_detected", False) for m in methods.values()
            ),
            "severity": self._aggregate_severity(
                [str(m.get("drift_severity", "none")) for m in methods.values()]
            ),
            "methods": methods,
        }

    def detect_feature_drift(
        self,
        reference_features: FeatureDict,
        current_features: FeatureDict,
    ) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Detect drift across multiple named features.

        Applies per-feature KS tests, pairwise correlation drift, and
        multivariate MMD drift (if arrays can be stacked).

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Feature name → baseline 1-D array.
        current_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Feature name → current 1-D array.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any method reports drift.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all methods.
            ``methods`` : dict[str, dict]
                Results for ``feature_drift``, ``correlation_drift``, and
                ``multivariate_drift``.
        """
        if not self.enable_feature:
            return {
                "drift_detected": False,
                "severity": "none",
                "message": "Feature drift detection disabled.",
            }

        methods: dict[str, DriftReport] = {
            "feature_drift": self._feat_drift.feature_drift_detector(
                reference_features, current_features
            ),
            "correlation_drift": self._feat_drift.correlation_drift(
                reference_features, current_features
            ),
        }

        try:
            feature_names = sorted(reference_features)
            ref_matrix = np.column_stack([reference_features[n] for n in feature_names])
            curr_matrix = np.column_stack([current_features[n] for n in feature_names])
            methods["multivariate_drift"] = self._feat_drift.multivariate_drift(
                ref_matrix, curr_matrix
            )
        except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
            logger.warning("Multivariate drift skipped")
            methods["multivariate_drift"] = {
                "drift_detected": False,
                "drift_severity": "none",
                "error": str(exc),
            }

        return self._build_aggregate_result(methods)

    def detect_prediction_drift(
        self,
        reference_predictions: np.ndarray,
        current_predictions: np.ndarray,
        reference_confidences: Optional[np.ndarray] = None,
        current_confidences: Optional[np.ndarray] = None,
    ) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Detect drift in model prediction outputs.

        Always runs prediction distribution shift detection. Optionally runs
        class imbalance change (for integer prediction arrays) and confidence
        drift (when confidence arrays are provided).

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_predictions : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D prediction score array.
        current_predictions : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D prediction score array.
        reference_confidences : np.ndarray or None, optional
            Baseline 1-D confidence score array. When provided together with
            ``current_confidences``, confidence drift is also computed.
        current_confidences : np.ndarray or None, optional
            Current 1-D confidence score array.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any method reports drift.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all methods.
            ``methods`` : dict[str, dict]
                Results for ``distribution_shift``, and optionally
                ``class_imbalance`` and ``confidence_drift``.
        """
        if not self.enable_prediction:
            return {
                "drift_detected": False,
                "severity": "none",
                "message": "Prediction drift detection disabled.",
            }

        methods: dict[str, DriftReport] = {
            "distribution_shift": self._pred_drift.prediction_distribution_shift(
                reference_predictions, current_predictions
            ),
        }

        if np.array_equal(reference_predictions, reference_predictions.astype(int)):
            methods["class_imbalance"] = self._pred_drift.class_imbalance_change(
                reference_predictions, current_predictions
            )

        if reference_confidences is not None and current_confidences is not None:
            methods["confidence_drift"] = self._pred_drift.prediction_confidence_drift(
                reference_confidences, current_confidences
            )

        return self._build_aggregate_result(methods)

    def detect_performance_drift(
        self,
        reference_metrics: MetricsDict,
        current_metrics: MetricsDict,
    ) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Detect drift in model performance metrics.

        Checks each supported metric key (``accuracy``, ``precision``,
        ``recall``, ``f1_score``, ``auc``) when present in both dicts, then
        computes an overall weighted degradation score.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_metrics : dict[str, float]
            Metric name → baseline value.
        current_metrics : dict[str, float]
            Metric name → current value.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any metric or the degradation score reports drift.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all methods.
            ``methods`` : dict[str, dict]
                Per-metric drift results plus ``degradation`` score.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If either metrics dict is empty.
        """
        if not self.enable_performance:
            return {
                "drift_detected": False,
                "severity": "none",
                "message": "Performance drift detection disabled.",
            }
        if not reference_metrics:
            raise ValueError("reference_metrics must not be empty.")
        if not current_metrics:
            raise ValueError("current_metrics must not be empty.")

        methods: dict[str, DriftReport] = {}

        for metric_key, method_key, method_fn in (
            ("accuracy", "accuracy", self._perf_drift.accuracy_drift),
            ("precision", "precision", self._perf_drift.precision_drift),
            ("recall", "recall", self._perf_drift.recall_drift),
            ("f1_score", "f1", self._perf_drift.f1_drift),
            ("auc", "auc", self._perf_drift.auc_drift),
        ):
            if metric_key in reference_metrics and metric_key in current_metrics:
                methods[method_key] = method_fn(
                    reference_metrics[metric_key], current_metrics[metric_key]
                )

        methods["degradation"] = self._perf_drift.performance_degradation_score(
            reference_metrics, current_metrics
        )

        return self._build_aggregate_result(methods)

    def detect_all_drifts(
        self,
        reference_data: dict[str, object],
        current_data: dict[str, object],
    ) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Run all enabled drift checks and return a unified report.

        Dispatches to ``detect_feature_drift``, ``detect_prediction_drift``,
        and ``detect_performance_drift`` based on which keys are present in the
        input dicts, then aggregates severity across all results.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : dict
            Must contain any combination of:
            ``"features"`` (``dict[str, np.ndarray]``),
            ``"predictions"`` (``np.ndarray``),
            ``"confidences"`` (``np.ndarray``, optional),
            ``"metrics"`` (``dict[str, float]``).
        current_data : dict
            Same structure as ``reference_data``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any drift category was detected.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all categories.
            ``drift_types`` : list[str]
                Names of categories where drift was detected (e.g.
                ``["feature", "prediction"]``).
            ``feature_drift`` : dict or None
                Result from ``detect_feature_drift``, or ``None`` if not run.
            ``prediction_drift`` : dict or None
                Result from ``detect_prediction_drift``, or ``None`` if not run.
            ``performance_drift`` : dict or None
                Result from ``detect_performance_drift``, or ``None`` if not run.
        """
        results: DriftReport = {
            "drift_detected": False,
            "severity": "none",
            "drift_types": [],
            "feature_drift": None,
            "prediction_drift": None,
            "performance_drift": None,
        }
        drift_types: list[str] = []

        if "features" in reference_data and "features" in current_data:
            fd = self.detect_feature_drift(
                reference_data["features"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
                current_data["features"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            )
            results["feature_drift"] = fd
            if fd.get("drift_detected"):
                drift_types.append("feature")

        if "predictions" in reference_data and "predictions" in current_data:
            pd_result = self.detect_prediction_drift(
                reference_data["predictions"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
                current_data["predictions"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
                reference_data.get("confidences"),  # type: ignore[arg-type]
                current_data.get("confidences"),  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            )
            results["prediction_drift"] = pd_result
            if pd_result.get("drift_detected"):
                drift_types.append("prediction")

        if "metrics" in reference_data and "metrics" in current_data:
            perf = self.detect_performance_drift(
                reference_data["metrics"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
                current_data["metrics"],  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            )
            results["performance_drift"] = perf
            if perf.get("drift_detected"):
                drift_types.append("performance")

        results["drift_types"] = drift_types
        results["drift_detected"] = len(drift_types) > 0
        results["severity"] = self._aggregate_severity(
            [
                str((results.get("feature_drift") or {}).get("severity", "none")),
                str((results.get("prediction_drift") or {}).get("severity", "none")),
                str((results.get("performance_drift") or {}).get("severity", "none")),
            ]
        )
        return results

    def generate_report(self, drift_results: DriftReport) -> str:
        """
        Generate a human-readable text report from drift detection results.

        Parameters
        ----------
        drift_results : dict
            Output of ``detect_all_drifts`` or any individual detection method.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            Multi-line text report showing overall drift status, severity,
            detected drift types, and per-category summaries.
        """
        lines: list[str] = []
        sep_major = "=" * 60
        sep_minor = "-" * 60
        lines.extend([sep_major, "DRIFT DETECTION REPORT", sep_major, ""])
        lines.append(f"Overall Drift Detected : {drift_results.get('drift_detected')}")
        lines.append(
            f"Severity               : {str(drift_results.get('severity', 'none')).upper()}"
        )
        drift_types = drift_results.get("drift_types", [])
        lines.append(
            f"Drift Types            : {', '.join(drift_types) if drift_types else 'None'}"
        )
        lines.append("")
        for section_key, label in (
            ("feature_drift", "FEATURE DRIFT"),
            ("prediction_drift", "PREDICTION DRIFT"),
            ("performance_drift", "PERFORMANCE DRIFT"),
        ):
            section = drift_results.get(section_key)
            if not section:
                continue
            lines.extend([sep_minor, label, sep_minor])
            lines.append(f"Detected : {section.get('drift_detected')}")
            lines.append(f"Severity : {section.get('severity')}")
            if section_key == "feature_drift":
                drifted = (
                    section.get("methods", {})
                    .get("feature_drift", {})
                    .get("drifted_features", [])
                )
                if drifted:
                    lines.append(f"Drifted Features : {', '.join(drifted)}")
            lines.append("")
        lines.append(sep_major)
        return "\n".join(lines)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Private helpers
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _aggregate_severity(severities: list[str]) -> str:
        """
        Return the highest severity from a list of severity strings.

        Parameters
        ----------
        severities : list[str]
            Severity labels (``"none"``, ``"low"``, ``"medium"``, ``"high"``,
            ``"critical"``).

        Returns
        -------
        str
            The maximum severity label.
        """
        max_rank = max((_SEVERITY_RANK.get(s, 0) for s in severities), default=0)
        return _RANK_SEVERITY.get(max_rank, "none")

    @staticmethod
    def _build_aggregate_result(methods: dict[str, DriftReport]) -> DriftReport:
        """
        Build an aggregated result dict from a set of per-method results.

        Parameters
        ----------
        methods : dict[str, dict]
            Mapping of method name to its result dict. Each result is expected
            to contain ``drift_detected`` and ``drift_severity`` keys.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any method reports drift.
            ``severity`` : str
                Maximum severity across all methods.
            ``methods`` : dict[str, dict]
                The original ``methods`` dict passed in.
        """
        drift_detected = any(
            bool(m.get("drift_detected", False)) for m in methods.values()
        )
        severity = DriftDetector._aggregate_severity(
            [str(m.get("drift_severity", "none")) for m in methods.values()]
        )
        return {
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "severity": severity,
            "methods": methods,
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\feature_drift.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Feature-level drift detection.

Provides per-feature statistical tests, multivariate drift via MMD/energy distance,
correlation structure drift, and feature importance rank drift.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import ClassVar, Literal

import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
from scipy.spatial.distance import cdist

from .statistical_tests import _validate_arrays

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

FeatureDict = dict[str, np.ndarray]
DriftResult = dict[str, object]


@dataclass
class FeatureDrift:
    """
    Feature-level drift detectors.

    All detection logic lives inside this class. Instantiate once with a
    threshold and call detection methods directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    threshold : float, optional
        Default p-value significance level / minimum change used when no
        per-call value is supplied. Must be a positive finite number.
        Default is ``0.05``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``threshold`` is not a positive finite number.
    """

    _EPSILON: ClassVar[float] = 1e-10

    threshold: float = field(default=0.05)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not np.isfinite(self.threshold) or self.threshold <= 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"threshold must be a positive finite number, got {self.threshold}."
            )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Private computation helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _compute_mmd(x: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray, gamma: float = 1.0) -> float:
        """
        Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) with RBF kernel.

        Parameters
        ----------
        x : np.ndarray
            Reference 2-D sample matrix of shape (m, n_features).
        y : np.ndarray
            Current 2-D sample matrix of shape (n, n_features).
        gamma : float, optional
            RBF kernel bandwidth parameter. Default is ``1.0``.

        Returns
        -------
        float
            ``sqrt(max(MMD², 0))`` — always non-negative.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If either array has fewer than 2 rows.
        """
        if x.shape[0] < 2 or y.shape[0] < 2:
            raise ValueError("MMD requires at least 2 samples in each array.")

        def _rbf(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
            aa = np.sum(a**2, axis=1)[:, np.newaxis]
            bb = np.sum(b**2, axis=1)[np.newaxis, :]
            return np.exp(-gamma * (aa + bb - 2.0 * (a @ b.T)))

        k_xx = _rbf(x, x)
        k_yy = _rbf(y, y)
        k_xy = _rbf(x, y)
        m, n = len(x), len(y)
        mmd = (k_xx.sum() - np.trace(k_xx)) / (m * (m - 1))
        mmd += (k_yy.sum() - np.trace(k_yy)) / (n * (n - 1))
        mmd -= 2.0 * k_xy.sum() / (m * n)
        return float(np.sqrt(max(mmd, 0.0)))

    @staticmethod
    def _compute_energy_distance(x: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray) -> float:
        """
        Energy distance between two multivariate samples.

        Parameters
        ----------
        x : np.ndarray
            Reference 2-D sample matrix of shape (m, n_features).
        y : np.ndarray
            Current 2-D sample matrix of shape (n, n_features).

        Returns
        -------
        float
            Energy distance (non-negative).
        """
        m, n = len(x), len(y)
        return float(
            2.0 * cdist(x, y).sum() / (m * n)
            - cdist(x, x).sum() / (m * m)
            - cdist(y, y).sum() / (n * n)
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public detection methods
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def feature_drift_detector(
        self,
        reference_features: FeatureDict,
        current_features: FeatureDict,
        threshold: float | None = None,
        method: Literal["ks", "chi2", "mw"] = "ks",
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Per-feature drift detection using a chosen statistical test.

        Iterates over every feature in ``reference_features``, applies the
        chosen test against the corresponding array in ``current_features``,
        and returns an aggregated result. Features missing from
        ``current_features`` are skipped with a warning.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Mapping of feature name to baseline 1-D array.
        current_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Mapping of feature name to current 1-D array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.
        method : {"ks", "chi2", "mw"}, optional
            Statistical test to use. ``"ks"`` = Kolmogorov-Smirnov (default),
            ``"chi2"`` = Chi-Square on 10-bin histogram, ``"mw"`` =
            Mann-Whitney U.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Feature Drift Detector"``.
            ``method`` : str
                Test method used.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``drifted_features`` : list[str]
                Names of features where drift was detected.
            ``num_drifted`` : int
                Number of drifted features.
            ``total_features`` : int
                Total number of reference features evaluated.
            ``feature_results`` : dict[str, dict]
                Per-feature ``statistic``, ``p_value``, ``drift_detected``.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when at least one feature drifted.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``reference_features`` is empty or ``method`` is unknown.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        if not reference_features:
            raise ValueError("reference_features must not be empty.")
        if method not in ("ks", "chi2", "mw"):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown method '{method}'. Choose from 'ks', 'chi2', 'mw'."
            )
        logger.debug(
            f"Running feature drift detector (method={method}) on {len(reference_features)} features."
        )
        drifted_features: list[str] = []
        feature_results: dict[str, dict[str, float | bool]] = {}
        for ftr_name, ref_data in reference_features.items():
            if ftr_name not in current_features:
                logger.warning(
                    "Feature '{ftr_name}' missing from current_features — skipped."
                )
                continue
            curr_data = current_features[ftr_name]
            try:
                _validate_arrays(ref_data, curr_data, ftr_name)
            except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
                logger.error("Invalid data for feature '{ftr_name}'")
                continue
            try:
                if method == "ks":
                    statistic, p_value = stats.ks_2samp(ref_data, curr_data)
                elif method == "mw":
                    statistic, p_value = stats.mannwhitneyu(
                        ref_data, curr_data, alternative="two-sided"
                    )
                else:
                    combined = np.concatenate([ref_data, curr_data])
                    edges = np.histogram_bin_edges(combined, bins=10)
                    ref_hist, _ = np.histogram(ref_data, bins=edges)
                    curr_hist, _ = np.histogram(curr_data, bins=edges)
                    ref_hist = (
                        (ref_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON)
                        / (ref_hist.sum() + self._EPSILON)
                        * curr_hist.sum()
                    )
                    statistic, p_value = stats.chisquare(
                        curr_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON, ref_hist
                    )
            except Exception as exc:
                logger.exception(
                    f"Statistical test failed for feature '{ftr_name}'"
                )
                continue
            drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
            if drift_detected:
                drifted_features.append(ftr_name)
            feature_results[ftr_name] = {
                "statistic": float(statistic),
                "p_value": float(p_value),
                "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            }
        return {
            "metric_name": "Feature Drift Detector",
            "method": method,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drifted_features": drifted_features,
            "num_drifted": len(drifted_features),
            "total_features": len(reference_features),
            "feature_results": feature_results,
            "drift_detected": len(drifted_features) > 0,
        }

    def multivariate_drift(
        self,
        reference_features: np.ndarray,
        current_features: np.ndarray,
        method: Literal["mmd", "energy"] = "mmd",
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Joint (multivariate) feature distribution drift via MMD or energy distance.

        Unlike univariate tests, this method examines all features simultaneously
        and can detect correlated shifts invisible to per-feature analysis.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_features : np.ndarray
            Baseline 2-D array of shape (n_samples, n_features).
        current_features : np.ndarray
            Current 2-D array of shape (n_samples, n_features).
        method : {"mmd", "energy"}, optional
            Distance measure. ``"mmd"`` = Maximum Mean Discrepancy with RBF
            kernel (default); ``"energy"`` = Energy Distance.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Multivariate Drift"``.
            ``method`` : str
                Distance method used.
            ``drift_score`` : float
                Computed distance (``√MMD²`` or energy distance).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Fixed internal threshold (0.1 for MMD, 1.0 for energy).
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``drift_score > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        TypeError
            If either input is not a ``numpy.ndarray``.
        ValueError
            If either array is not 2-D, has fewer than 2 rows, or ``method``
            is unknown.
        """
        for label, arr in (
            ("reference", reference_features),
            ("current", current_features),
        ):
            if not isinstance(arr, np.ndarray):
                raise TypeError(f"'{label}' must be a numpy.ndarray.")
            if arr.ndim != 2:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"'{label}' must be 2-dimensional, got shape {arr.shape}."
                )
            if arr.shape[0] < 2:
                raise ValueError(f"'{label}' must have at least 2 rows.")
        if method not in ("mmd", "energy"):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown method '{method}'. Choose from 'mmd' or 'energy'."
            )
        logger.debug("Running multivariate drift (method=%s).", method)
        if method == "mmd":
            drift_score = self._compute_mmd(reference_features, current_features)
            threshold = 0.1
        else:
            drift_score = self._compute_energy_distance(
                reference_features, current_features
            )
            threshold = 1.0
        drift_detected = bool(drift_score > threshold)
        if drift_score < threshold * 0.5:
            severity = "low"
        elif drift_score < threshold:
            severity = "medium"
        elif drift_score < threshold * 2.0:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Multivariate Drift",
            "method": method,
            "drift_score": float(drift_score),
            "threshold": float(threshold),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def correlation_drift(
        self,
        reference_features: FeatureDict,
        current_features: FeatureDict,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect pairwise feature correlation structure drift.

        Computes Pearson correlation matrices for reference and current data,
        then flags feature pairs whose absolute correlation change exceeds
        ``threshold``.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Mapping of feature name to baseline 1-D array.
        current_features : dict[str, np.ndarray]
            Mapping of feature name to current 1-D array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute correlation change to flag a pair. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Correlation Drift"``.
            ``max_correlation_change`` : float
                Maximum absolute pairwise correlation change.
            ``mean_correlation_change`` : float
                Mean absolute pairwise correlation change.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``max_correlation_change > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``changed_pairs`` : list[dict]
                Details for each feature pair exceeding ``threshold``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If fewer than 2 common features exist or arrays have inconsistent
            lengths.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        common_names = sorted(set(reference_features) & set(current_features))
        if len(common_names) < 2:
            raise ValueError(
                f"At least 2 common features required for correlation drift, got {len(common_names)}."
            )
        logger.debug("Computing correlation drift over %d features.", len(common_names))
        try:
            ref_matrix = np.column_stack([reference_features[n] for n in common_names])
            curr_matrix = np.column_stack([current_features[n] for n in common_names])
        except ValueError as exc:
            raise ValueError("Feature arrays must have consistent lengths.") from exc
        ref_corr = np.corrcoef(ref_matrix.T)
        curr_corr = np.corrcoef(curr_matrix.T)
        corr_diff = np.abs(ref_corr - curr_corr)
        upper_idx = np.triu_indices_from(corr_diff, k=1)
        corr_changes = corr_diff[upper_idx]
        max_change = float(np.max(corr_changes))
        mean_change = float(np.mean(corr_changes))
        drift_detected = bool(max_change > t)
        changed_pairs = [
            {
                "feature1": common_names[i],
                "feature2": common_names[j],
                "ref_correlation": float(ref_corr[i, j]),
                "curr_correlation": float(curr_corr[i, j]),
                "change": float(corr_diff[i, j]),
            }
            for i, j in zip(*upper_idx)
            if corr_diff[i, j] > t
        ]
        if max_change < 0.1:
            severity = "low"
        elif max_change < 0.2:
            severity = "medium"
        elif max_change < 0.3:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Correlation Drift",
            "max_correlation_change": max_change,
            "mean_correlation_change": mean_change,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
            "changed_pairs": changed_pairs,
        }

    def feature_importance_drift(
        self,
        reference_importances: dict[str, float],
        current_importances: dict[str, float],
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect feature importance value and ranking changes.

        Flags individual features whose importance changed by more than
        ``threshold`` and computes Spearman rank correlation between the
        reference and current importance rankings.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_importances : dict[str, float]
            Mapping of feature name to baseline importance score.
        current_importances : dict[str, float]
            Mapping of feature name to current importance score.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute importance change to flag a feature. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Feature Importance Drift"``.
            ``rank_correlation`` : float
                Spearman rank correlation of importances in [-1, 1].
            ``significant_changes`` : list[str]
                Feature names with importance change exceeding ``threshold``.
            ``importance_changes`` : dict[str, dict]
                Per-feature ``reference``, ``current``, and ``change`` values.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when any feature changed significantly or
                ``rank_correlation < 0.8``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If either importances dict is empty.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        if not reference_importances:
            raise ValueError("reference_importances must not be empty.")
        if not current_importances:
            raise ValueError("current_importances must not be empty.")
        logger.debug("Computing feature importance drift.")
        importance_changes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
        significant_changes: list[str] = []
        for name, ref_imp in reference_importances.items():
            if name not in current_importances:
                continue
            curr_imp = current_importances[name]
            change = abs(curr_imp - ref_imp)
            importance_changes[name] = {
                "reference": float(ref_imp),
                "current": float(curr_imp),
                "change": float(change),
            }
            if change > t:
                significant_changes.append(name)
        common = sorted(set(reference_importances) & set(current_importances))
        if len(common) > 1:
            rank_corr = float(
                stats.spearmanr(
                    [reference_importances[f] for f in common],
                    [current_importances[f] for f in common],
                )[0]
            )
        else:
            rank_corr = 1.0
        drift_detected = bool(len(significant_changes) > 0 or rank_corr < 0.8)
        if rank_corr > 0.9:
            severity = "low"
        elif rank_corr > 0.8:
            severity = "medium"
        elif rank_corr > 0.7:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Feature Importance Drift",
            "rank_correlation": rank_corr,
            "significant_changes": significant_changes,
            "importance_changes": importance_changes,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\performance_drift.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Performance drift detection for ML models.

Detects degradation in classification and regression metrics by comparing
reference (baseline) values against current (production) values.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

DriftResult = dict[str, float | bool | str | dict[str, float]]


@dataclass
class PerformanceDrift:
    """
    Performance drift detector for ML model metrics.

    All detection logic lives inside this class. Instantiate once with a
    threshold and call metric methods directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    threshold : float, optional
        Default minimum absolute change required to flag drift. Must be a
        positive finite number. Default is ``0.05``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``threshold`` is not a positive finite number.
    """

    _EPSILON: ClassVar[float] = 1e-10

    threshold: float = field(default=0.05)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not np.isfinite(self.threshold) or self.threshold <= 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"threshold must be a positive finite number, got {self.threshold}."
            )

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_metric_value(value: float, name: str) -> None:
        if not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
            raise TypeError(
                f"'{name}' must be a numeric value, got {type(value).__name__}."
            )
        if not np.isfinite(value):
            raise ValueError(f"'{name}' must be a finite number, got {value}.")

    @staticmethod
    def _severity(abs_change: float) -> str:
        if abs_change < 0.02:
            return "low"
        if abs_change < 0.05:
            return "medium"
        if abs_change < 0.10:
            return "high"
        return "critical"

    def _scalar_drift(
        self,
        metric_name: str,
        result_key: str,
        reference: float,
        current: float,
        threshold: float,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        change = current - reference
        abs_change = abs(change)
        drift_detected = bool(abs_change > threshold)
        return {
            "metric_name": metric_name,
            "reference_value": float(reference),
            "current_value": float(current),
            result_key: float(change),
            "threshold": float(threshold),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": self._severity(abs_change) if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def accuracy_drift(
        self,
        reference_accuracy: float,
        current_accuracy: float,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in model accuracy.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_accuracy : float
            Baseline accuracy value in [0, 1].
        current_accuracy : float
            Current accuracy value in [0, 1].
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Accuracy Drift"``.
            ``reference_value`` : float
                Baseline accuracy.
            ``current_value`` : float
                Current accuracy.
            ``accuracy_change`` : float
                Signed change (current − reference).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``|accuracy_change| > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        self._validate_metric_value(reference_accuracy, "reference_accuracy")
        self._validate_metric_value(current_accuracy, "current_accuracy")
        logger.debug(
            "Checking accuracy drift: %.4f -> %.4f",
            reference_accuracy,
            current_accuracy,
        )
        return self._scalar_drift(
            "Accuracy Drift", "accuracy_change", reference_accuracy, current_accuracy, t
        )

    def precision_drift(
        self,
        reference_precision: float,
        current_precision: float,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in model precision.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_precision : float
            Baseline precision value in [0, 1].
        current_precision : float
            Current precision value in [0, 1].
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Precision Drift"``.
            ``reference_value`` : float
                Baseline precision.
            ``current_value`` : float
                Current precision.
            ``precision_change`` : float
                Signed change (current − reference).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``|precision_change| > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        self._validate_metric_value(reference_precision, "reference_precision")
        self._validate_metric_value(current_precision, "current_precision")
        return self._scalar_drift(
            "Precision Drift",
            "precision_change",
            reference_precision,
            current_precision,
            t,
        )

    def recall_drift(
        self,
        reference_recall: float,
        current_recall: float,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in model recall.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_recall : float
            Baseline recall value in [0, 1].
        current_recall : float
            Current recall value in [0, 1].
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Recall Drift"``.
            ``reference_value`` : float
                Baseline recall.
            ``current_value`` : float
                Current recall.
            ``recall_change`` : float
                Signed change (current − reference).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``|recall_change| > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        self._validate_metric_value(reference_recall, "reference_recall")
        self._validate_metric_value(current_recall, "current_recall")
        return self._scalar_drift(
            "Recall Drift", "recall_change", reference_recall, current_recall, t
        )

    def f1_drift(
        self,
        reference_f1: float,
        current_f1: float,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in model F1 score.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_f1 : float
            Baseline F1 score in [0, 1].
        current_f1 : float
            Current F1 score in [0, 1].
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"F1 Score Drift"``.
            ``reference_value`` : float
                Baseline F1 score.
            ``current_value`` : float
                Current F1 score.
            ``f1_change`` : float
                Signed change (current − reference).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``|f1_change| > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        self._validate_metric_value(reference_f1, "reference_f1")
        self._validate_metric_value(current_f1, "current_f1")
        return self._scalar_drift(
            "F1 Score Drift", "f1_change", reference_f1, current_f1, t
        )

    def auc_drift(
        self,
        reference_auc: float,
        current_auc: float,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in AUC-ROC score.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_auc : float
            Baseline AUC-ROC value in [0, 1].
        current_auc : float
            Current AUC-ROC value in [0, 1].
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"AUC Drift"``.
            ``reference_value`` : float
                Baseline AUC-ROC.
            ``current_value`` : float
                Current AUC-ROC.
            ``auc_change`` : float
                Signed change (current − reference).
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``|auc_change| > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        self._validate_metric_value(reference_auc, "reference_auc")
        self._validate_metric_value(current_auc, "current_auc")
        return self._scalar_drift(
            "AUC Drift", "auc_change", reference_auc, current_auc, t
        )

    def performance_degradation_score(
        self,
        reference_metrics: dict[str, float],
        current_metrics: dict[str, float],
        weights: dict[str, float] | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Weighted composite performance degradation score.

        Accumulates only negative metric changes (degradations). Improvements
        are treated as zero contribution. Optional per-metric weights allow
        prioritising critical metrics.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_metrics : dict[str, float]
            Mapping of metric name to baseline value.
        current_metrics : dict[str, float]
            Mapping of metric name to current value.
        weights : dict[str, float] or None, optional
            Per-metric weights. When ``None``, all metrics are equally weighted.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Performance Degradation Score"``.
            ``degradation_score`` : float
                Weighted degradation. < 0.02 low, 0.02–0.05 medium,
                0.05–0.10 high, > 0.10 critical.
            ``individual_degradations`` : dict[str, float]
                Per-metric degradation contributions.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``degradation_score >= 0.02``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If either metrics dict is empty, they share no common keys, or the
            sum of weights is zero.
        """
        if not reference_metrics:
            raise ValueError("reference_metrics must not be empty.")
        if not current_metrics:
            raise ValueError("current_metrics must not be empty.")
        common_keys = set(reference_metrics) & set(current_metrics)
        if not common_keys:
            raise ValueError(
                "reference_metrics and current_metrics share no common keys."
            )
        effective_weights = (
            weights if weights is not None else {k: 1.0 for k in reference_metrics}
        )
        total_weight = sum(effective_weights.values())
        if total_weight == 0:
            raise ValueError("Sum of weights must not be zero.")
        norm_weights = {k: v / total_weight for k, v in effective_weights.items()}
        degradation_scores: dict[str, float] = {}
        total_degradation = 0.0
        for key in common_keys:
            change = (current_metrics[key] - reference_metrics[key]) / (
                reference_metrics[key] + self._EPSILON
            )
            degradation = -change if change < 0.0 else 0.0
            degradation_scores[key] = degradation
            total_degradation += degradation * norm_weights.get(key, 0.0)
        if total_degradation < 0.02:
            severity, drift_detected = "low", False
        elif total_degradation < 0.05:
            severity, drift_detected = "medium", True
        elif total_degradation < 0.10:
            severity, drift_detected = "high", True
        else:
            severity, drift_detected = "critical", True
        logger.debug(
            "Performance degradation score: %.4f (%s)", total_degradation, severity
        )
        return {
            "metric_name": "Performance Degradation Score",
            "degradation_score": float(total_degradation),
            "individual_degradations": degradation_scores,
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity,
        }

    def metric_stability_check(
        self,
        metric_history: list[float],
        window_size: int = 5,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Temporal stability check via rolling coefficient of variation and trend.

        Analyses the most recent ``window_size`` values from a metric history to
        detect instability (high variance or monotonic trend).

        Parameters
        ----------
        metric_history : list[float]
            Ordered list of historical metric values (oldest first).
        window_size : int, optional
            Number of recent values to analyse. Must be >= 2. Default is ``5``.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            CV / trend instability threshold. Falls back to ``self.threshold``
            when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Metric Stability"``.
            ``stable`` : bool
                ``True`` when both CV and trend are below ``threshold``.
            ``coefficient_of_variation`` : float
                CV of the recent window (std / mean).
            ``trend`` : float
                Linear slope fitted to the recent window.
            ``mean`` : float
                Mean of the recent window.
            ``std`` : float
                Std of the recent window.
            ``instability_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``metric_history`` is empty or ``window_size < 2``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        if not metric_history:
            raise ValueError("metric_history must not be empty.")
        if window_size < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"window_size must be >= 2, got {window_size}.")
        if len(metric_history) < window_size:
            logger.debug(
                "Insufficient data for stability check (%d < %d).",
                len(metric_history),
                window_size,
            )
            return {
                "metric_name": "Metric Stability",
                "stable": True,
                "coefficient_of_variation": 0.0,
                "trend": 0.0,
                "mean": float(np.mean(metric_history)),
                "std": float(np.std(metric_history)),
                "instability_severity": "none",
                "message": "Insufficient data for stability check.",
            }
        recent = metric_history[-window_size:]
        mean_val = float(np.mean(recent))
        std_val = float(np.std(recent))
        cv = std_val / (mean_val + self._EPSILON)
        trend = float(np.polyfit(np.arange(len(recent)), recent, 1)[0])
        stable = bool(cv < t and abs(trend) < t)
        if cv < 0.05:
            severity = "low"
        elif cv < 0.1:
            severity = "medium"
        elif cv < 0.2:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Metric Stability",
            "stable": stable,
            "coefficient_of_variation": cv,
            "trend": trend,
            "mean": mean_val,
            "std": std_val,
            "instability_severity": severity if not stable else "none",
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\prediction_drift.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Prediction drift detection.

Detects shifts in prediction distributions, class imbalance changes, confidence
drift, error distribution changes, and systematic prediction bias.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import ClassVar, Optional

import numpy as np
from scipy import stats

from .statistical_tests import _validate_arrays

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

DriftResult = dict[str, object]


@dataclass
class PredictionDrift:
    """
    Prediction drift detectors for ML model outputs.

    All detection logic lives inside this class. Instantiate once with a
    threshold and call detection methods directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    threshold : float, optional
        Default significance level / minimum change used when no per-call
        value is supplied. Must be a positive finite number. Default is
        ``0.05``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``threshold`` is not a positive finite number.
    """

    _EPSILON: ClassVar[float] = 1e-10

    threshold: float = field(default=0.05)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not np.isfinite(self.threshold) or self.threshold <= 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"threshold must be a positive finite number, got {self.threshold}."
            )

    def prediction_distribution_shift(
        self,
        reference_predictions: np.ndarray,
        current_predictions: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect prediction score distribution shift via KS test.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_predictions : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D prediction score array.
        current_predictions : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D prediction score array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Prediction Distribution Shift"``.
            ``ks_statistic`` : float
                KS test statistic.
            ``p_value`` : float
                KS test p-value.
            ``prediction_dist_shift`` : float
                Alias of ``ks_statistic``.
            ``mean_shift`` : float
                Absolute change in mean prediction score.
            ``std_shift`` : float
                Absolute change in std of prediction scores.
            ``reference_mean`` : float
                Mean of reference predictions.
            ``current_mean`` : float
                Mean of current predictions.
            ``reference_std`` : float
                Std of reference predictions.
            ``current_std`` : float
                Std of current predictions.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``p_value < threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(
            reference_predictions, current_predictions, "prediction_distribution_shift"
        )
        logger.debug("Checking prediction distribution shift.")
        statistic, p_value = stats.ks_2samp(reference_predictions, current_predictions)
        drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
        ref_mean = float(np.mean(reference_predictions))
        curr_mean = float(np.mean(current_predictions))
        ref_std = float(np.std(reference_predictions))
        curr_std = float(np.std(current_predictions))
        if statistic < 0.1:
            severity = "low"
        elif statistic < 0.2:
            severity = "medium"
        elif statistic < 0.3:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Prediction Distribution Shift",
            "ks_statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "prediction_dist_shift": float(statistic),
            "mean_shift": abs(curr_mean - ref_mean),
            "std_shift": abs(curr_std - ref_std),
            "reference_mean": ref_mean,
            "current_mean": curr_mean,
            "reference_std": ref_std,
            "current_std": curr_std,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def class_imbalance_change(
        self,
        reference_predictions: np.ndarray,
        current_predictions: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
        num_classes: Optional[int] = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect per-class proportion changes for classification models.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_predictions : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D integer class label array.
        current_predictions : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D integer class label array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum proportion change to flag a class as drifted. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.
        num_classes : int or None, optional
            Number of classes. Inferred from the data when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Class Imbalance Change"``.
            ``max_proportion_change`` : float
                Maximum absolute proportion change across all classes.
            ``mean_proportion_change`` : float
                Mean absolute proportion change.
            ``changed_classes`` : list[dict]
                Per-class details for classes whose proportion changed beyond
                ``threshold``.
            ``reference_proportions`` : list[float]
                Per-class proportions in the reference window.
            ``current_proportions`` : list[float]
                Per-class proportions in the current window.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``max_proportion_change > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``num_classes < 1``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(
            reference_predictions, current_predictions, "class_imbalance_change"
        )
        if num_classes is None:
            num_classes = int(
                len(
                    np.unique(
                        np.concatenate([reference_predictions, current_predictions])
                    )
                )
            )
        elif num_classes < 1:
            raise ValueError(f"num_classes must be >= 1, got {num_classes}.")
        logger.debug("Checking class imbalance change over %d classes.", num_classes)
        ref_props = np.bincount(
            reference_predictions.astype(int), minlength=num_classes
        ) / len(reference_predictions)
        curr_props = np.bincount(
            current_predictions.astype(int), minlength=num_classes
        ) / len(current_predictions)
        prop_changes = np.abs(curr_props - ref_props)
        max_change = float(np.max(prop_changes))
        drift_detected = bool(max_change > t)
        changed_classes = [
            {
                "class": int(i),
                "reference_proportion": float(ref_props[i]),
                "current_proportion": float(curr_props[i]),
                "change": float(prop_changes[i]),
            }
            for i in range(num_classes)
            if prop_changes[i] > t
        ]
        if max_change < 0.05:
            severity = "low"
        elif max_change < 0.1:
            severity = "medium"
        elif max_change < 0.2:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Class Imbalance Change",
            "max_proportion_change": max_change,
            "mean_proportion_change": float(np.mean(prop_changes)),
            "changed_classes": changed_classes,
            "reference_proportions": ref_props.tolist(),
            "current_proportions": curr_props.tolist(),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def prediction_confidence_drift(
        self,
        reference_confidences: np.ndarray,
        current_confidences: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in prediction confidence / probability scores.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_confidences : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D confidence score array (values in [0, 1]).
        current_confidences : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D confidence score array (values in [0, 1]).
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum mean confidence change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Prediction Confidence Drift"``.
            ``mean_confidence_change`` : float
                Absolute change in mean confidence.
            ``std_confidence_change`` : float
                Absolute change in confidence std.
            ``reference_mean_confidence`` : float
                Mean of reference confidence scores.
            ``current_mean_confidence`` : float
                Mean of current confidence scores.
            ``reference_std_confidence`` : float
                Std of reference confidence scores.
            ``current_std_confidence`` : float
                Std of current confidence scores.
            ``low_confidence_ref`` : float
                Fraction of reference predictions with confidence < 0.5.
            ``low_confidence_curr`` : float
                Fraction of current predictions with confidence < 0.5.
            ``high_confidence_ref`` : float
                Fraction of reference predictions with confidence > 0.9.
            ``high_confidence_curr`` : float
                Fraction of current predictions with confidence > 0.9.
            ``ks_statistic`` : float
                KS statistic between confidence distributions.
            ``p_value`` : float
                KS p-value.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``mean_confidence_change > threshold`` or
                ``p_value < 0.05``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(
            reference_confidences, current_confidences, "prediction_confidence_drift"
        )
        logger.debug("Checking prediction confidence drift.")
        ref_mean = float(np.mean(reference_confidences))
        curr_mean = float(np.mean(current_confidences))
