        ref_std = float(np.std(reference_confidences))
        curr_std = float(np.std(current_confidences))
        mean_change = abs(curr_mean - ref_mean)
        statistic, p_value = stats.ks_2samp(reference_confidences, current_confidences)
        drift_detected = bool(mean_change > t or p_value < 0.05)
        if mean_change < 0.02:
            severity = "low"
        elif mean_change < 0.05:
            severity = "medium"
        elif mean_change < 0.1:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Prediction Confidence Drift",
            "mean_confidence_change": float(mean_change),
            "std_confidence_change": abs(curr_std - ref_std),
            "reference_mean_confidence": ref_mean,
            "current_mean_confidence": curr_mean,
            "reference_std_confidence": ref_std,
            "current_std_confidence": curr_std,
            "low_confidence_ref": float(
                np.sum(reference_confidences < 0.5) / len(reference_confidences)
            ),
            "low_confidence_curr": float(
                np.sum(current_confidences < 0.5) / len(current_confidences)
            ),
            "high_confidence_ref": float(
                np.sum(reference_confidences > 0.9) / len(reference_confidences)
            ),
            "high_confidence_curr": float(
                np.sum(current_confidences > 0.9) / len(current_confidences)
            ),
            "ks_statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def prediction_error_drift(
        self,
        reference_errors: np.ndarray,
        current_errors: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect drift in prediction error distribution (regression).

        Compares MAE and RMSE of residuals between the reference and current
        windows using both absolute and relative changes.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_errors : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D residual array (prediction − actual).
        current_errors : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D residual array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum relative change (fraction) to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Prediction Error Drift"``.
            ``mae_change`` : float
                Absolute change in MAE.
            ``rmse_change`` : float
                Absolute change in RMSE.
            ``mae_relative_change`` : float
                MAE change relative to reference MAE.
            ``rmse_relative_change`` : float
                RMSE change relative to reference RMSE.
            ``reference_mae`` / ``current_mae`` : float
                Reference and current MAE values.
            ``reference_rmse`` / ``current_rmse`` : float
                Reference and current RMSE values.
            ``ks_statistic`` : float
                KS statistic on absolute residuals.
            ``p_value`` : float
                KS p-value.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when relative MAE or RMSE change exceeds ``threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(reference_errors, current_errors, "prediction_error_drift")
        logger.debug("Checking prediction error drift.")
        ref_mae = float(np.mean(np.abs(reference_errors)))
        curr_mae = float(np.mean(np.abs(current_errors)))
        ref_rmse = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(reference_errors**2)))
        curr_rmse = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(current_errors**2)))
        mae_change = abs(curr_mae - ref_mae)
        rmse_change = abs(curr_rmse - ref_rmse)
        mae_rel = mae_change / (ref_mae + self._EPSILON)
        rmse_rel = rmse_change / (ref_rmse + self._EPSILON)
        statistic, p_value = stats.ks_2samp(
            np.abs(reference_errors), np.abs(current_errors)
        )
        drift_detected = bool(mae_rel > t or rmse_rel > t)
        max_rel = max(mae_rel, rmse_rel)
        if max_rel < 0.1:
            severity = "low"
        elif max_rel < 0.2:
            severity = "medium"
        elif max_rel < 0.5:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Prediction Error Drift",
            "mae_change": float(mae_change),
            "rmse_change": float(rmse_change),
            "mae_relative_change": float(mae_rel),
            "rmse_relative_change": float(rmse_rel),
            "reference_mae": ref_mae,
            "current_mae": curr_mae,
            "reference_rmse": ref_rmse,
            "current_rmse": curr_rmse,
            "ks_statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def prediction_bias_drift(
        self,
        reference_predictions: np.ndarray,
        reference_actuals: np.ndarray,
        current_predictions: np.ndarray,
        current_actuals: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Detect change in systematic prediction bias (mean signed error).

        Bias is defined as the mean of (prediction − actual). A change in bias
        indicates that the model has become systematically over- or under-predicting.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_predictions : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D prediction array.
        reference_actuals : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D ground-truth array.
        current_predictions : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D prediction array.
        current_actuals : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D ground-truth array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            Minimum absolute bias change to flag drift. Falls back to
            ``self.threshold`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Prediction Bias Drift"``.
            ``bias_change`` : float
                Absolute change in bias (|current_bias − reference_bias|).
            ``reference_bias`` : float
                Mean signed error in the reference window.
            ``current_bias`` : float
                Mean signed error in the current window.
            ``reference_relative_bias`` : float
                Reference bias normalised by reference actual std.
            ``current_relative_bias`` : float
                Current bias normalised by current actual std.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Threshold used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``bias_change > threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(
            reference_predictions,
            reference_actuals,
            "reference (predictions vs actuals)",
        )
        _validate_arrays(
            current_predictions, current_actuals, "current (predictions vs actuals)"
        )
        logger.debug("Checking prediction bias drift.")
        ref_bias = float(np.mean(reference_predictions - reference_actuals))
        curr_bias = float(np.mean(current_predictions - current_actuals))
        bias_change = abs(curr_bias - ref_bias)
        drift_detected = bool(bias_change > t)
        if bias_change < 0.02:
            severity = "low"
        elif bias_change < 0.05:
            severity = "medium"
        elif bias_change < 0.1:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "metric_name": "Prediction Bias Drift",
            "bias_change": float(bias_change),
            "reference_bias": ref_bias,
            "current_bias": curr_bias,
            "reference_relative_bias": ref_bias
            / (float(np.std(reference_actuals)) + self._EPSILON),
            "current_relative_bias": curr_bias
            / (float(np.std(current_actuals)) + self._EPSILON),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\stability_metrics.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Stability metrics for drift detection: PSI, CSI, and DSI.

These metrics quantify the degree to which a feature's distribution has shifted
compared to a reference baseline, and are widely used in production ML monitoring.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
from scipy import stats

from .statistical_tests import _validate_arrays

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

DriftResult = dict[str, float | bool | str]


@dataclass
class StabilityMetrics:
    """
    Stability metrics (PSI, CSI, DSI) for distribution drift detection.

    Instantiate once with a default bin count and call metric methods
    directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bins : int, optional
        Default number of histogram bins used when no per-call value is
        supplied. Must be >= 2. Default is ``10``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``bins < 2``.
    """

    _EPSILON: ClassVar[float] = 1e-10

    bins: int = field(default=10)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.bins < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {self.bins}.")

    @staticmethod
    def _psi_interpretation(psi: float) -> str:
        if psi < 0.1:
            return "No significant change detected."
        if psi < 0.2:
            return "Moderate change detected — investigate further."
        return "Significant change detected — consider model retraining."

    @staticmethod
    def _psi_severity(psi: float) -> tuple[str, bool]:
        if psi < 0.1:
            return "low", False
        if psi < 0.2:
            return "medium", True
        if psi < 0.3:
            return "high", True
        return "critical", True

    def population_stability_index(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
        bin_edges: np.ndarray | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Population Stability Index (PSI).

        Industry-standard metric for measuring distribution shift. Widely used
        in credit risk modelling and financial services ML monitoring.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.
        bin_edges : np.ndarray or None, optional
            Pre-computed bin edges. When provided, ``bins`` is ignored and
            these edges are used directly.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Population Stability Index"``.
            ``psi_score`` : float
                PSI value. < 0.1 no change, 0.1–0.2 moderate, > 0.2 significant.
            ``bins`` : int
                Number of bins used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``psi_score >= 0.1``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
            ``interpretation`` : str
                Human-readable PSI interpretation string.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "population_stability_index")
        logger.debug("Computing PSI with %d bins.", b)
        edges = (
            bin_edges
            if bin_edges is not None
            else np.histogram_bin_edges(reference_data, bins=b)
        )
        actual_bins = len(edges) - 1
        ref_hist, _ = np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)
        curr_hist, _ = np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)
        ref_pct = (ref_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON) / (
            ref_hist.sum() + actual_bins * self._EPSILON
        )
        curr_pct = (curr_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON) / (
            curr_hist.sum() + actual_bins * self._EPSILON
        )
        psi = float(np.sum((curr_pct - ref_pct) * np.log(curr_pct / ref_pct)))
        severity, drift_detected = self._psi_severity(psi)
        return {
            "metric_name": "Population Stability Index",
            "psi_score": psi,
            "bins": actual_bins,
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity,
            "interpretation": self._psi_interpretation(psi),
        }

    def characteristic_stability_index(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Characteristic Stability Index (CSI).

        Measures the maximum relative bin deviation between distributions.
        More sensitive to tail shifts than PSI.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int or None, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Falls back to ``self.bins`` when ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Characteristic Stability Index"``.
            ``csi_score`` : float
                Maximum relative bin deviation. < 0.1 stable, 0.1–0.25 moderate,
                0.25–0.5 high, > 0.5 critical.
            ``bins`` : int
                Number of bins used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``csi_score >= 0.1``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        b = bins if bins is not None else self.bins
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "characteristic_stability_index")
        logger.debug("Computing CSI with %d bins.", b)
        edges = np.histogram_bin_edges(reference_data, bins=b)
        ref_hist, _ = np.histogram(reference_data, bins=edges)
        curr_hist, _ = np.histogram(current_data, bins=edges)
        ref_pct = (ref_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON) / (
            ref_hist.sum() + b * self._EPSILON
        )
        curr_pct = (curr_hist.astype(float) + self._EPSILON) / (
            curr_hist.sum() + b * self._EPSILON
        )
        csi = float(np.max(np.abs((curr_pct - ref_pct) / ref_pct)))
        if csi < 0.1:
            severity, drift_detected = "low", False
        elif csi < 0.25:
            severity, drift_detected = "medium", True
        elif csi < 0.5:
            severity, drift_detected = "high", True
        else:
            severity, drift_detected = "critical", True
        return {
            "metric_name": "Characteristic Stability Index",
            "csi_score": csi,
            "bins": b,
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity,
        }

    def data_stability_index(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Data Stability Index (DSI).

        Composite of normalised mean, variance, skewness and kurtosis shifts.
        Provides a single score summarising overall distributional change.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``metric_name`` : str
                Always ``"Data Stability Index"``.
            ``dsi_score`` : float
                Composite DSI. < 0.5 stable, 0.5–1.0 moderate, 1.0–2.0 high,
                > 2.0 critical.
            ``mean_shift`` : float
                Normalised mean shift (|Δμ| / σ_ref).
            ``variance_shift`` : float
                Normalised std shift (|Δσ| / σ_ref).
            ``skewness_shift`` : float
                Absolute change in skewness.
            ``kurtosis_shift`` : float
                Absolute change in excess kurtosis, scaled by 1/10.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``dsi_score >= 0.5``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "data_stability_index")
        logger.debug("Computing DSI.")
        ref_std = float(np.std(reference_data))
        mean_shift = abs(
            float(np.mean(current_data)) - float(np.mean(reference_data))
        ) / (ref_std + self._EPSILON)
        variance_shift = abs(float(np.std(current_data)) - ref_std) / (
            ref_std + self._EPSILON
        )
        skew_shift = abs(
            float(stats.skew(current_data)) - float(stats.skew(reference_data))
        )
        kurt_shift = (
            abs(
                float(stats.kurtosis(current_data))
                - float(stats.kurtosis(reference_data))
            )
            / 10.0
        )
        dsi = mean_shift + variance_shift + skew_shift + kurt_shift
        if dsi < 0.5:
            severity, drift_detected = "low", False
        elif dsi < 1.0:
            severity, drift_detected = "medium", True
        elif dsi < 2.0:
            severity, drift_detected = "high", True
        else:
            severity, drift_detected = "critical", True
        return {
            "metric_name": "Data Stability Index",
            "dsi_score": float(dsi),
            "mean_shift": float(mean_shift),
            "variance_shift": float(variance_shift),
            "skewness_shift": float(skew_shift),
            "kurtosis_shift": float(kurt_shift),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity,
        }


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\ml\drift\statistical_tests.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Statistical hypothesis tests for drift detection.

Provides KS, Chi-Square, Anderson-Darling and Mann-Whitney U tests
to detect distributional shifts between reference and current datasets.
"""

import logging
import warnings
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
from scipy import stats

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

DriftResult = dict[str, float | bool | str | None]


def _validate_arrays(
    reference: np.ndarray, current: np.ndarray, name: str = "data"
) -> None:
    """
    Validate that both arrays are non-empty 1-D numpy arrays.

    Shared validation helper imported by all drift detection modules.

    Parameters
    ----------
    reference : np.ndarray
        Baseline data array.
    current : np.ndarray
        Current data array.
    name : str, optional
        Label used in error messages to identify the calling context.

    Raises
    ------
    TypeError
        If either input is not a ``numpy.ndarray``.
    ValueError
        If either array is not 1-dimensional or is empty.
    """
    for label, arr in (("reference", reference), ("current", current)):
        if not isinstance(arr, np.ndarray):
            raise TypeError(
                f"{name} '{label}' must be a numpy.ndarray, got {type(arr).__name__}."
            )
        if arr.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{name} '{label}' must be 1-dimensional, got shape {arr.shape}."
            )
        if arr.size == 0:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} '{label}' must not be empty.")


@dataclass
class StatisticalTests:
    """
    Statistical hypothesis tests for distribution drift detection.

    All test logic is implemented inside this class. Instantiate once with a
    significance threshold and call test methods directly on the instance.

    Parameters
    ----------
    threshold : float, optional
        Default p-value significance level used when no per-call threshold is
        supplied. Must satisfy ``0 < threshold < 1``. Default is ``0.05``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``threshold`` is not in the open interval (0, 1).
    """

    threshold: float = field(default=0.05)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 0.0 < self.threshold < 1.0:
            raise ValueError(f"threshold must be in (0, 1), got {self.threshold}.")

    @staticmethod
    def _ks_severity(statistic: float) -> str:
        if statistic < 0.1:
            return "low"
        if statistic < 0.2:
            return "medium"
        if statistic < 0.3:
            return "high"
        return "critical"

    def kolmogorov_smirnov_test(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov test for distribution drift.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``test_name`` : str
                Always ``"Kolmogorov-Smirnov"``.
            ``statistic`` : float
                KS test statistic D (maximum absolute CDF difference).
            ``p_value`` : float
                Two-sided p-value.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``p_value < threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "kolmogorov_smirnov_test")
        logger.debug(
            "Running KS test on %d vs %d samples.",
            len(reference_data),
            len(current_data),
        )
        statistic, p_value = stats.ks_2samp(reference_data, current_data)
        drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
        return {
            "test_name": "Kolmogorov-Smirnov",
            "statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": self._ks_severity(float(statistic))
            if drift_detected
            else "none",
        }

    def chi_square_test(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        bins: int = 10,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Chi-Square goodness-of-fit test on binned data.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        bins : int, optional
            Number of histogram bins. Must be >= 2. Default is ``10``.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``test_name`` : str
                Always ``"Chi-Square"``.
            ``statistic`` : float
                Chi-Square test statistic.
            ``p_value`` : float
                p-value of the test.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``bins`` : int
                Number of bins used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``p_value < threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``bins < 2``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "chi_square_test")
        if bins < 2:
            raise ValueError(f"bins must be >= 2, got {bins}.")
        logger.debug("Running Chi-Square test with %d bins.", bins)
        combined = np.concatenate([reference_data, current_data])
        bin_edges = np.histogram_bin_edges(combined, bins=bins)
        ref_freq, _ = np.histogram(reference_data, bins=bin_edges)
        curr_freq, _ = np.histogram(current_data, bins=bin_edges)
        ref_freq = ref_freq.astype(float) + 1e-10
        curr_freq = curr_freq.astype(float) + 1e-10
        ref_freq = ref_freq / ref_freq.sum() * curr_freq.sum()
        statistic, p_value = stats.chisquare(curr_freq, ref_freq)
        drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
        if statistic < bins:
            severity = "low"
        elif statistic < bins * 2:
            severity = "medium"
        elif statistic < bins * 3:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "test_name": "Chi-Square",
            "statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "threshold": float(t),
            "bins": int(bins),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def anderson_darling_test(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Anderson-Darling two-sample test via ``scipy.stats.anderson_ksamp``.

        More sensitive to distribution tails than the KS test.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``test_name`` : str
                Always ``"Anderson-Darling"``.
            ``statistic`` : float
                Anderson-Darling test statistic.
            ``critical_value`` : float
                Critical value at the chosen significance level.
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``p_value < threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "anderson_darling_test")
        logger.debug("Running Anderson-Darling test.")
        with warnings.catch_warnings():
            warnings.simplefilter("ignore", UserWarning)
            result = stats.anderson_ksamp([reference_data, current_data])
        statistic = float(result.statistic)
        p_value = float(result.pvalue)
        _std_levels = np.array([0.25, 0.10, 0.05, 0.025, 0.01, 0.005, 0.001])
        if hasattr(result, "critical_values"):
            _cv_array = np.asarray(result.critical_values)
        else:
            _cv_array = np.array([0.325, 1.226, 1.961, 2.718, 3.752, 4.592, 6.546])
        closest_idx = int(np.argmin(np.abs(_std_levels - t)))
        critical_value = float(_cv_array[closest_idx])
        drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
        if statistic < 0.5:
            severity = "low"
        elif statistic < 1.0:
            severity = "medium"
        elif statistic < 2.0:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "test_name": "Anderson-Darling",
            "statistic": statistic,
            "critical_value": critical_value,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

    def mann_whitney_test(
        self,
        reference_data: np.ndarray,
        current_data: np.ndarray,
        threshold: float | None = None,
    ) -> DriftResult:
        """
        Mann-Whitney U non-parametric two-sample test.

        Suitable for non-normal distributions and ordinal data.

        Parameters
        ----------
        reference_data : np.ndarray
            Baseline 1-D sample array.
        current_data : np.ndarray
            Current 1-D sample array.
        threshold : float or None, optional
            p-value significance level. Falls back to ``self.threshold`` when
            ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        dict
            ``test_name`` : str
                Always ``"Mann-Whitney U"``.
            ``statistic`` : float
                Mann-Whitney U statistic.
            ``p_value`` : float
                Two-sided p-value.
            ``effect_size`` : float
                Common language effect size in [-1, 1].
            ``threshold`` : float
                Significance level used.
            ``drift_detected`` : bool
                ``True`` when ``p_value < threshold``.
            ``drift_severity`` : str
                ``"none"`` | ``"low"`` | ``"medium"`` | ``"high"`` | ``"critical"``.
        """
        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
        _validate_arrays(reference_data, current_data, "mann_whitney_test")
        logger.debug("Running Mann-Whitney U test.")
        statistic, p_value = stats.mannwhitneyu(
            reference_data, current_data, alternative="two-sided"
        )
        drift_detected = bool(p_value < t)
        n1, n2 = len(reference_data), len(current_data)
        effect_size = float(1.0 - (2.0 * statistic) / (n1 * n2))
        abs_effect = abs(effect_size)
        if abs_effect < 0.1:
            severity = "low"
        elif abs_effect < 0.3:
            severity = "medium"
        elif abs_effect < 0.5:
            severity = "high"
        else:
            severity = "critical"
        return {
            "test_name": "Mann-Whitney U",
            "statistic": float(statistic),
            "p_value": float(p_value),
            "effect_size": effect_size,
            "threshold": float(t),
            "drift_detected": drift_detected,
            "drift_severity": severity if drift_detected else "none",
        }

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\utils\_cml_api.py

import logging
from datetime import datetime
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

import cmlapi
from cmlapi import CMLServiceApi, CreateJobRequest, models

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

RUNTIME_IDENTIFIER = [
    "hadoop-cli-7.3.1.200-90",
    # This equals to spark 3.5.1; to change spark version probably create dummy job with
    # respective spark version & then use CML Job api to fetch details
    "spark351-7218800-1240-b711",
]
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 60 * 10


class CAIClient:
    """Client for interacting with Cloudera AI (CAI) API.

    Parameters
    ----------
    project_id : str
        The CML project identifier.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="my-project-123")

    >>> job_config = client.job_config_template(
    ...     job_prefix_name="data-processing",
    ...     batch_no=1,
    ...     job_script="src/jobs/process_data.py"
    ... )
    >>> job = client.create_job(job_config)
    >>> job_run = client.create_job_run(job.id)
    """

    project_id: str

    @property
    def client(self) -> CMLServiceApi:
        """Get the CML service API client.

        Returns
        -------
        CMLServiceApi
            The default CML API client instance.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> cai = CAIClient()
        >>> api_client = cai.client
        """
        _ = cmlapi.default_client()
        return _

    def job_config_template(
        self,
        job_prefix_name: str,
        batch_no: int | None,
        job_script: str,
        cpu: int = 2,
        memory: int = 8,
        env: dict | None = None,
        runtime_identifier: str = "nexus.dlhdevuat.sbi.bank.in:9443/clouderadataservices/cloudera/cdsw/ml-runtime-jupyterlab-python3.11-ml:2026.03.03.v1",
    ) -> CreateJobRequest:
        """Create a CML job configuration template.

        Generates a job configuration with specified resources and runtime settings.
        If batch_no is provided, it will be included in the job name along with
        the current timestamp.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_prefix_name : str
            Prefix for the job name.
        batch_no : int or None
            Batch number to include in job name. If None, only prefix is used.
        job_script : str
            Path to the script file to execute (e.g., "src/jobs/process_data.py").
        cpu : int, optional
            Number of CPU cores to allocate, by default 2.
        memory : int, optional
            Memory in GB to allocate, by default 8.
        env : dict or None, optional
            Environment variables as key-value pairs, by default None.
        runtime_identifier : str, optional
            Docker image identifier for the runtime environment.

        Returns
        -------
        CreateJobRequest
            Configured job request object ready for submission.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> # Create job config without batch number
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="etl-pipeline",
        ...     batch_no=None,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/etl.py",
        ...     cpu=4,
        ...     memory=16
        ... )
        >>>
        >>> # Create job config with batch number
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="data-processing",
        ...     batch_no=5,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/process.py",
        ...     env={"ENV": "production", "LOG_LEVEL": "INFO"}
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        - The job timeout is set to 10 minutes (600 seconds).
        - Jobs are configured to be killed on timeout.
        - Spark runtime addons are automatically included for Spark 3.5.1.
        """
        # If there are batches then only we'll have batch name in job name
        name = (
            f"{job_prefix_name}-batch-{batch_no}-{str(datetime.now(tz=ZoneInfo('Asia/Kolkata')))}"
            if batch_no
            else job_prefix_name
        )

        return cmlapi.CreateJobRequest(
            name=name,
            project_id=self.project_id,
            script=job_script,
            cpu=cpu,
            memory=memory,
            environment=env,
            # NOTE - runtime should match with intended runtime docker image
            runtime_identifier=runtime_identifier,
            runtime_addon_identifiers=RUNTIME_IDENTIFIER,
            timeout=DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
            kill_on_timeout=True,
        )

    def create_job(self, job_config: CreateJobRequest) -> models.job.Job:
        """Create a new CML job.

        Submits a job configuration to CML and creates the job in the project.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_config : CreateJobRequest
            Job configuration object created by job_config_template().

        Returns
        -------
        models.job.Job
            The created job object with job ID and metadata.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")

        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="ml-training",
        ...     batch_no=1,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/train_model.py"
        ... )
        >>> job = client.create_job(config)
        >>> print(f"Job created with ID: {job.id}")
        Job created with ID: job-abc-123

        See Also
        --------
        job_config_template : Create job configuration
        create_job_run : Execute a created job
        """
        job = self.client.create_job(job_config, project_id=self.project_id)
        logger.info(f"successfully created job with {job.id}")
        return job

    def create_job_run(self, job_id: str) -> models.job_run.JobRun:
        """Execute a CML job by creating a job run.

        Creates and starts a new run for an existing job.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_id : str
            The unique identifier of the job to run.

        Returns
        -------
        models.job_run.JobRun
            The created job run object with run ID and status.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> # First create a job
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="data-pipeline",
        ...     batch_no=None,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/pipeline.py"
        ... )
        >>> job = client.create_job(config)
        >>>
        >>> # Then execute the job
        >>> job_run = client.create_job_run(job.id)
        >>> print(f"Job run started with ID: {job_run.id}")
        Job run started with ID: run-xyz-456

        See Also
        --------
        create_job : Create a new job
        _job_run_template : Generate job run configuration
        """
        job_run_config = self._job_run_template(job_id=job_id)
        run = self.client.create_job_run(
            job_run_config, job_id=job_id, project_id=setting.common.project_id
        )
        logger.info(f"successfully created job run {run.id}")
        return run

    def _job_run_template(self, job_id: str):
        """Create a job run configuration template.

        Internal method to generate the configuration for executing a job.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_id : str
            The unique identifier of the job to run.

        Returns
        -------
        cmlapi.CreateJobRunRequest
            Job run request configuration object.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> run_config = client._job_run_template(job_id="job-abc-123")
        """
        return cmlapi.CreateJobRunRequest(project_id=self.project_id, job_id=job_id)


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\utils\_hdfs_client.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import os
import subprocess
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass

import pyarrow.dataset as ds
import pyarrow.fs as fs

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class HDFSClient:
    """HDFS client for interacting with Hadoop Distributed File System.

    This client provides methods for file operations, data reading, and model loading
    from HDFS using PyArrow filesystem interface and HDFS CLI commands.

    Parameters
    ----------

    host : str, default="default"
        HDFS host connection string. Use "default" to connect to the default
        filesystem configured in Hadoop settings.


    Examples
    --------

    >>> # Initialize HDFS client with default host
    >>> client = HDFSClient()

    >>> # Initialize with specific host (rarely needed)
    >>> client = HDFSClient(host="hdfs://namenode:8020")

    >>> # List files in a directory
    >>> files = client.list_files("/user/some_folder/data")

    >>> # Upload a file to HDFS
    >>> client.upload("local_file.csv", "/user/data/remote_file.csv")

    >>> # Download a file from HDFS
    >>> client.download("/user/data/remote_file.csv", "local_file.csv")

    >>> # Read parquet data
    >>> dataset = client.read_parquet_table("/user/data/parquet_files")
    """

    host: str = "default"

    def __post_init__(self):
        # configuring env variable
        logger.debug("setting env variable for hdfs client")
        os.environ["CLASSPATH"] = os.popen("hdfs classpath").read().strip()
        # TODO - Below line is for cloudera cluster & needs to be handle in better way
        os.environ["HADOOP_OPTS"] = (
            "-Djavax.net.ssl.trustStore=/var/lib/cloudera-scm-agent/agent-cert/cm-auto-global_truststore.jks -Djavax.net.ssl.trustStorePassword=Sbi@123"
        )

    @property
    def hdfs_filesystem(self) -> fs.HadoopFileSystem:
        """Get PyArrow HadoopFileSystem instance.

        Returns
        -------
        fs.HadoopFileSystem
            Configured HadoopFileSystem instance for HDFS operations.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> filesystem = client.hdfs_filesystem
        >>> file_info = filesystem.get_file_info("/user/data")
        """
        config = {
            "ssl.client.truststore.location": "/var/lib/cloudera-scm-agent/agent-cert/cm-auto-global_truststore.jks",
            "ssl.client.truststore.password": "Sbi@123",
            "ssl.client.truststore.type": "jks",
            "hadoop.rpc.socket.factory.class.default": "org.apache.hadoop.net.StandardSocketFactory"
        }
        _ = fs.HadoopFileSystem(host=self.host, extra_conf=config)
        return _

    def list_files(self, path: str) -> list[fs.FileInfo]:
        """List all files in the specified HDFS path recursively.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            HDFS path to list files from.

        Returns
        -------
        list[fs.FileInfo]
            List of FileInfo objects containing file metadata.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> files = client.list_files("/user/data")
        >>> for file in files:
        ...     print(f"{file.path}: {file.size} bytes")
        """
        selector = fs.FileSelector(path, recursive=True)
        files = self.hdfs_filesystem.get_file_info(selector)
        return [file for file in files]

    def upload(self, source_path: str, destination_path: str, overwrite: bool = True):
        """Upload a file from local filesystem to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            Local file path to upload.
        destination_path : str
            HDFS destination path.
        overwrite : bool, optional
            Overwrite existing file if it exists. Default is True.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> client.upload("local_data.csv", "/user/data/remote_data.csv")
        >>>
        >>> # Upload without force overwrite
        >>> client.upload("model.pkl", "/user/models/model.pkl", overwrite=False)

        Notes
        -----
        Uses HDFS CLI command with -f flag to force overwrite if file exists.
        PyArrow filesystem is not used due to authentication issues.
        """
        # TODO - Ideally pyarrow.fs should be used but currently there is
        # a issue with auth & only hdfs cli is working
        if overwrite:
            subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-put", "-f", source_path, destination_path])
        else:
            subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-put", source_path, destination_path])

    def download(self, source_path: str, destination_path: str):
        """Download a file from HDFS to local filesystem.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS file path to download.
        destination_path : str
            Local destination path.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> client.download("/user/data/remote_data.csv", "local_data.csv")
        >>>
        >>> # Download to specific directory
        >>> client.download("/user/models/model.pkl", "./models/model.pkl")

        Notes
        -----
        Uses HDFS CLI command for downloading.
        PyArrow filesystem is not used due to authentication issues.
        """
        # TODO - Ideally pyarrow.fs should be used but currently there is
        # a issue with auth & only hdfs cli is working
        subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-get", source_path, destination_path])

    def read_parquet_table(self, source_path: str) -> ds.FileSystemDataset:
        """Read parquet files from HDFS as a PyArrow dataset.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS path to parquet file or directory containing parquet files.

        Returns
        -------
        ds.FileSystemDataset
            PyArrow FileSystemDataset for the parquet data.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> dataset = client.read_parquet_table("/user/data/parquet_files")
        >>>
        >>> # Convert to pandas DataFrame
        >>> df = dataset.to_table().to_pandas()
        >>>
        >>> # Read with filters
        >>> table = dataset.to_table(filter=ds.field("year") == 2024)
        >>> df = table.to_pandas()
        """
        return ds.dataset(
            source=source_path, filesystem=self.hdfs_filesystem, format="parquet"
        )

    def read_csv_file(self, source_path: str) -> ds.FileSystemDataset:
        """Read CSV files from HDFS as a PyArrow dataset.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS path to CSV file or directory containing CSV files.

        Returns
        -------
        ds.FileSystemDataset
            PyArrow FileSystemDataset for the CSV data.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> dataset = client.read_csv_file("/user/data/csv_files")
        """
        return ds.dataset(
            source=source_path, filesystem=self.hdfs_filesystem, format="csv"
        )

    def load_model_sentence_transformer(self, model_path: str, **kwargs):
        """Load a SentenceTransformer model from HDFS.

        Downloads the model from HDFS to a temporary directory and loads it
        using the sentence-transformers library.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model_path : str
            HDFS path to the SentenceTransformer model directory.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to SentenceTransformer constructor.

        Returns
        -------
        SentenceTransformer
            Loaded SentenceTransformer model instance.

        Raises
        ------
        ImportError
            If sentence-transformers library is not installed.
        ValueError
            If model requires trust_remote_code=True (automatically handled).

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> model = client.load_model_sentence_transformer(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/google/embeddinggemma4"
        ... )
        >>> embeddings = model.encode(["Hello world", "How are you?"])

        >>> # Load with onnx device backend
        >>> model = client.load_model_sentence_transformer(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/google/embeddinggemma4",
        ...     backend="onnx"
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        The model is downloaded to a temporary directory which is automatically
        cleaned up after loading. If the model requires trust_remote_code=True,
        it will be automatically enabled with a warning.
        """
        try:
            from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
        except ImportError:
            raise ImportError(
                "`sentence-transformers` library is not installed, please install it"
            )

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_model_path = f"{temp_dir}/model_dir"
            logger.debug("downloading the model from hdfs")
            self.download(model_path, local_model_path)
            logger.debug(
                f"successfully downloaded model temporarily to: {local_model_path}"
            )
            try:
                logger.info(f"loading model from {local_model_path}")
                model = SentenceTransformer(local_model_path, **kwargs)
            except ValueError as e:
                if "trust_remote_code=True" in str(e):
                    logger.warning(
                        "the model need to trust & execute custom code, enabling it"
                    )
                    model = SentenceTransformer(
                        local_model_path, trust_remote_code=True, **kwargs
                    )
                else:
                    raise e

        return model

    def load_model_cross_encoder(self, model_path: str, **kwargs):
        """Load a CrossEncoder model from HDFS.

        Downloads the model from HDFS to a temporary directory and loads it
        using the sentence-transformers library.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model_path : str
            HDFS path to the CrossEncoder model directory.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to CrossEncoder constructor.

        Returns
        -------
        CrossEncoder
            Loaded CrossEncoder model instance.

        Raises
        ------
        ImportError
            If sentence-transformers library is not installed.
        ValueError
            If model requires trust_remote_code=True (automatically handled).

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> model = client.load_model_cross_encoder(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/jina/jina-reranker-multilingual-base-v2"
        ... )
        >>> scores = model.predict([
        ...     ("Query 1", "Document 1"),
        ...     ("Query 2", "Document 2")
        ... ])
        >>>
        >>> # Load with custom settings
        >>> model = client.load_model_cross_encoder(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/jina/jina-reranker-multilingual-base-v2",
        ...     max_length=512
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        The model is downloaded to a temporary directory which is automatically
        cleaned up after loading. If the model requires trust_remote_code=True,
        it will be automatically enabled with a warning.
        """
        try:
            from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder
        except ImportError:
            raise ImportError(
                "`sentence-transformers` library is not installed, please install it"
            )

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_model_path = f"{temp_dir}/model_dir"
            logger.debug("downloading the model from hdfs")
            self.download(model_path, local_model_path)
            logger.debug(
                f"successfully downloaded model temporarily to: {local_model_path}"
            )
            try:
                logger.info(f"loading model from {local_model_path}")
                model = CrossEncoder(local_model_path, **kwargs)
            except ValueError as e:
                if "trust_remote_code=True" in str(e):
                    logger.warning(
                        "the model need to trust & execute custom code, enabling it"
                    )
                    model = CrossEncoder(
                        local_model_path, trust_remote_code=True, **kwargs
                    )
                else:
                    raise e

        return model


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\jobs\Index.md
####################################################################################################



## 1. Overview
The system utilizes a Vector Database (Elasticsearch) to enable semantic search and conversational memory for the AI Chatbot. Unlike traditional keyword search, this architecture uses **Dense Vector Embeddings** to find information based on the *meaning* of the query rather than exact word matches.

The database is divided into three primary indices, each serving a specific purpose in the RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) pipeline:

1.  **FAQ Knowledge Base**: The "Source of Truth." Stores embedded documents for semantic retrieval.
2.  **Chat Sessions**: The "Conversation Memory." Stores the history of user interactions.
3.  **LLM Cache**: The "Performance Layer." Stores previous query-answer pairs to reduce LLM costs and latency.

---


**Purpose:** This index stores the core knowledge. When a user asks a question, the system converts that question into a vector and performs a **Cosine Similarity** search against the `embedding` field.

| Field Name | Data Type | Description | Role |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `faq_id` | Keyword | Unique identifier for the FAQ | Primary Key |
| `faq_version` | Integer | Versioning for document updates | Metadata |
| `category` | Keyword | The business category (e.g., Retail, Loans) | Filtering |
| `type` | Keyword | Type of FAQ | Filtering |
| `question` | Text | The original user question | Searchable Text |
| `answer` | Text | The official response | Content |
| `combined` | Text | Concatenated Category + Question + Answer | Embedding Source |
| **`embedding`** | **Dense Vector** | Numerical representation of the `combined` text | **Vector Search** |
| `created_at` | Date | Timestamp of creation | Audit |
| `updated_at` | Date | Timestamp of last update | Audit |

**Vector Config:**
- **Similarity Metric:** Cosine Similarity (measures the angle between two vectors).
- **Dimensions:** Defined by the embedding model (e.g., 768 or 1536).

---

**Purpose:** This index manages the state of the conversation. It allows the bot to remember what was said earlier in the session to maintain context.

| Field Name | Data Type | Description | Role |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `session_id` | Keyword | Unique ID for the chat session | Primary Key |
| `created_at` | Date | Session start time | Metadata |
| `updated_at` | Date | Last message time | Metadata |
| `message` | **Nested** | A list of messages within the session | Chat History |
| `message.role` | Keyword | Who sent the message (`user` or `assistant`) | Role |
| `message.text` | Text | The actual text of the message | Content |
| `retrieved_docs`| **Nested** | IDs of FAQ docs used to generate this answer | Traceability |
| `timestamp` | Date | Exact time of the message | Sequence |

---

**Purpose:** To optimize performance. If a new question is semantically identical to a previous one, the system retrieves the cached answer instead of calling the LLM again.

| Field Name | Data Type | Description | Role |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `cache_id` | Keyword | Unique identifier for the cache entry | Primary Key |
| `cache_hash` | Keyword | Hash of the normalized question | Exact Match |
| `normalized_question`| Text | The cleaned-up version of the user query | Searchable Text |
| **`query_embedding`** | **Dense Vector** | Vector representation of the user query | **Semantic Cache Hit** |
| `knowledge_snapshot`| Flattened | Snapshot of the knowledge used at that time | Version Control |
| `answer` | Text | The LLM generated response | Cached Result |
| `stale` | Boolean | Flag indicating if the cache is outdated | Logic |
| `hit_count` | Integer | How many times this cache was used | Analytics |
| `last_cache_hit` | Date | Last time this answer was reused | TTL/Cleanup |

---


| Feature | FAQ Index | Chat Session Index | Cache Index |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Search Type** | Semantic (Vector) | Exact (ID) | Hybrid (Hash + Vector) |
| **Primary Goal** | Knowledge Retrieval | Context Persistence | Latency Reduction |
| **Vector Space** | Document Embeddings | N/A | Query Embeddings |
| **Update Frequency**| Low (Admin updates) | High (Per message) | Medium (Per new query) |


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\jobs\faq_ingention_job.py
####################################################################################################



import logging
import time
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch, helpers
import httpx
import mlflow
from mlflow.entities import RunStatus
import pandas as pd

from dlh_ai.config import get_settings

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

EMBEDDING_ENDPOINT = "/api/v1/embedding/large"

REQUIRED_CSV_COLUMNS = ["Sno", "Category", "Question", "Answer", "Type"]


def get_embeddings(http_client: httpx.Client, texts: list[str]) -> list[list[float]]:
    """Embed a batch of texts via the large embedding service.

    Parameters
    ----------
    http_client : httpx.Client
        Client pre-configured with the embedding service's base url.
    texts : list[str]
        Texts to embed.

    Returns
    -------
    list[list[float]]
        One embedding vector per input text, in the same order.
    """
    response = http_client.post(EMBEDDING_ENDPOINT, json={"texts": texts})
    response.raise_for_status()
    return response.json()["embeddings"]


def _build_combined_text(category: str, question: str, answer: str) -> str:
    """Build the text blob that gets embedded for semantic search."""
    return f"Category: {category}. Question: {question} Answer: {answer}"


def ingest_faq_csv(
    client: Elasticsearch,
    http_client: httpx.Client,
    csv_file_path: str,
    index_name: str,
    embedding_dimension: int,
    batch_size: int = 100,
) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Read the FAQ CSV, embed each row via the embedding service and bulk index it into Elasticsearch.

    Assumes `index_name` already exists with a mapping compatible with
    `api.models.FaqDocument` (created via UI or differnt job, not by this job).

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        Elasticsearch client.
    http_client : httpx.Client
        Client used to call the large embedding service for the `combined` text.
    csv_file_path : str
        Path to the FAQ knowledge base CSV file.
    index_name : str
        Target Elasticsearch index.
    embedding_dimension : int
        Expected embedding dimension, validated against the service's response.
    batch_size : int, optional
        Number of rows embedded & bulk-indexed per request, by default 100.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple[int, int]
        Count of (successfully indexed, failed) documents.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the embedding service returns vectors of an unexpected dimension.
    """
    logger.info(f"reading faq csv: {csv_file_path}")
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_file_path)
    logger.info(f"csv loaded with {len(df)} rows")

    missing_columns = [c for c in REQUIRED_CSV_COLUMNS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing_columns:
        raise ValueError(f"missing csv columns: {missing_columns}")

    # category is only populated on the first row of each group in the source sheet
    df["Category"] = df["Category"].ffill().fillna("")
    df["Question"] = df["Question"].fillna("")
    df["Answer"] = df["Answer"].fillna("")
    df["Type"] = df["Type"].fillna("")

    successful = 0
    failed = 0
    total_batches = -(-len(df) // batch_size)  # ceil division

    for batch_num, start in enumerate(range(0, len(df), batch_size), start=1):
        logger.info(f"ingesting batch {batch_num}/{total_batches}")
        batch_df = df.iloc[start : start + batch_size]

        texts = [
            _build_combined_text(
                str(row["Category"]).strip(),
                str(row["Question"]).strip(),
                str(row["Answer"]).strip(),
            )
            for _, row in batch_df.iterrows()
        ]
        embeddings = get_embeddings(http_client, texts)

        if batch_num == 1 and len(embeddings[0]) != embedding_dimension:
            raise ValueError(
                f"expected embedding dimension {embedding_dimension}, "
                f"but embedding service returned {len(embeddings[0])}"
            )

        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        faq_ids = [f"faq-{int(row['Sno'])}" for _, row in batch_df.iterrows()]
        existing_docs = client.mget(index=index_name, ids=faq_ids).get("docs", [])
        existing_by_id = {
            doc["_id"]: doc.get("_source", {})
            for doc in existing_docs
            if doc.get("found")
        }
        actions = []
        for (_, row), embedding, combined_text in zip(
            batch_df.iterrows(), embeddings, texts
        ):
            # keyed on Sno so re-running the job upserts instead of duplicating docs
            faq_id = f"faq-{int(row['Sno'])}"
            existing = existing_by_id.get(faq_id, {})
            content_changed = any(
                existing.get(field) != value
                for field, value in {
                    "category": str(row["Category"]).strip(),
                    "type": str(row["Type"]).strip(),
                    "question": str(row["Question"]).strip(),
                    "answer": str(row["Answer"]).strip(),
                    "combined": combined_text,
                }.items()
            )
            current_version = int(existing.get("faq_version", 0))
            faq_version = current_version + 1 if current_version and content_changed else max(current_version, 1)
            actions.append(
                {
                    "_index": index_name,
                    "_id": faq_id,
                    "_source": {
                        "faq_id": faq_id,
                        "faq_version": faq_version,
                        "category": str(row["Category"]).strip(),
                        "type": str(row["Type"]).strip(),
                        "question": str(row["Question"]).strip(),
                        "answer": str(row["Answer"]).strip(),
                        "combined": combined_text,
                        "embedding": embedding,
                        "created_at": now,
                        "updated_at": now,
                    },
                }
            )

        try:
            success, errors = helpers.bulk(client, actions, raise_on_error=False)
            successful += success
            failed += len(errors)
        except Exception:
            logger.exception(f"bulk indexing failed for batch starting at row {start}")
            failed += len(actions)

    logger.info(f"ingestion completed: {successful} succeeded, {failed} failed")
    return successful, failed


settings = get_settings()

client = Elasticsearch(
    settings.elastic.host,
    api_key=settings.elastic.api_key,
    request_timeout=30,
    retry_on_timeout=True,
    verify_certs=settings.elastic.verify_certs,
)

embedding_http_client = httpx.Client(
    base_url=settings.elastic.embedding_service_url, timeout=60, verify=settings.elastic.verify_certs
)

mlflow.set_experiment(settings.elastic.mlflow_experiment_name)

start_time = time.time()
exception_occurred = None  # Initialize this BEFORE try block

try:
    run = mlflow.start_run()
    mlflow.set_tags({"kind": "ingestion", "component": "faq_knowledge_base"})
    mlflow.log_params(
        {
            "csv_file_path": settings.elastic.csv_file_path,
            "index_name": settings.elastic.faq_index_name,
            "batch_size": settings.elastic.batch_size,
            "embedding_dimension": settings.elastic.embedding_dimension,
        }
    )
    logger.info(f"started faq ingestion mlflow run: {run.info.run_id}")

    cluster_info = client.info()
    logger.info(
        f"connected to elasticsearch cluster '{cluster_info['cluster_name']}' "
        f"version {cluster_info['version']['number']}"
    )

    successful, failed = ingest_faq_csv(
        client=client,
        http_client=embedding_http_client,
        csv_file_path=settings.elastic.csv_file_path,
        index_name=settings.elastic.faq_index_name,
        embedding_dimension=settings.elastic.embedding_dimension,
        batch_size=settings.elastic.batch_size,
    )
    mlflow.log_metrics({"documents_indexed": successful, "documents_failed": failed})

    client.indices.refresh(index=settings.elastic.faq_index_name)
    indexed_count = client.count(index=settings.elastic.faq_index_name)["count"]
    mlflow.log_metric("index_document_count", indexed_count)
    logger.info(f"index '{settings.elastic.faq_index_name}' now contains {indexed_count} documents")

    if failed:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"{failed} documents failed to index into '{settings.elastic.faq_index_name}'"
        )

    mlflow_status = RunStatus.to_string(RunStatus.FINISHED)
    end_time = time.time()
    elapsed_time = end_time - start_time
    logger.info(f"finished faq ingestion job in {elapsed_time:.2f} seconds")

except Exception as e:
    exception_occurred = e  # Save exception for re-raising
    mlflow_status = RunStatus.to_string(RunStatus.FAILED)
    mlflow.log_text(str(e), "error.log")
    logger.exception("faq ingestion job failed", exc_info=e)

finally:
    embedding_http_client.close()
    mlflow.end_run(mlflow_status)
    logger.info("closed faq ingestion mlflow run")

if exception_occurred:
    raise exception_occurred


####################################################################################################
FILE: src\jobs\index_creation_job.py
####################################################################################################



import logging
import time

from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch
import mlflow
from mlflow.entities import RunStatus

from dlh_ai.config import get_settings

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


def build_faq_index_mapping(embedding_dimension: int) -> dict:
    """Build the FAQ knowledge base index mapping, matching `api.models.FaqDocument`."""
    return {
        "mappings": {
            "properties": {
                "faq_id": {"type": "keyword"},
                "faq_version": {"type": "integer"},
                "category": {"type": "keyword"},
                "type": {"type": "keyword"},
                "question": {"type": "text"},
                "answer": {"type": "text"},
                "combined": {"type": "text"},
                "embedding": {
                    "type": "dense_vector",
                    "dims": embedding_dimension,
                    "similarity": "cosine",
                },
                "created_at": {"type": "date"},
                "updated_at": {"type": "date"},
            }
        }
    }


def build_chat_sessions_index_mapping() -> dict:
    """Build the chat sessions index mapping, matching `api.models.ChatSession`."""
    return {
        "mappings": {
            "properties": {
                "session_id": {"type": "keyword"},
                "created_at": {"type": "date"},
                "updated_at": {"type": "date"},
                "message": {
                    "type": "nested",
                    "properties": {
                        "id": {"type": "integer"},
                        "role": {"type": "keyword"},
                        "text": {"type": "text"},
                        "retrieved_docs": {
                            "type": "nested",
                            "properties": {
                                "faq_id": {"type": "keyword"},
                                "faq_version": {"type": "integer"},
                            },
                        },
                        "timestamp": {"type": "date"},
                    },
                },
            }
        }
    }


def build_llm_cache_index_mapping(embedding_dimension: int) -> dict:
    """Build the LLM cache index mapping, matching `api.models.LlmCacheResponse`."""
    return {
        "mappings": {
            "properties": {
                "cache_id": {"type": "keyword"},
                "cache_hash": {"type": "keyword"},
                "normalized_question": {"type": "text"},
                "query_embedding": {
                    "type": "dense_vector",
                    "dims": embedding_dimension,
                    "similarity": "cosine",
                },
                "knowledge_snapshot": {"type": "flattened"},
                "answer": {"type": "text"},
                "stale": {"type": "boolean"},
                "hit_count": {"type": "integer"},
                "created_at": {"type": "date"},
                "last_cache_hit": {"type": "date"},
            }
        }
    }


def create_index(client: Elasticsearch, index_name: str, mapping: dict) -> bool:
    """Create `index_name` with `mapping` if it doesn't already exist.

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        Elasticsearch client.
    index_name : str
        Index to create.
    mapping : dict
        Index body, as returned by one of the `build_*_index_mapping` functions.

    Returns
    -------
    bool
        True if the index was created, False if it already existed.
    """
    if client.indices.exists(index=index_name):
        logger.info(f"index '{index_name}' already exists, skipping creation")
        return False

    logger.info(f"creating index '{index_name}'")
    client.indices.create(index=index_name, body=mapping)
    logger.info(f"created index '{index_name}'")
    return True


settings = get_settings()

client = Elasticsearch(
    settings.elastic.host,
    api_key=settings.elastic.api_key,
    request_timeout=30,
    retry_on_timeout=True,
    verify_certs=settings.elastic.verify_certs,
)

mlflow.set_experiment(settings.elastic.mlflow_experiment_name)

start_time = time.time()
exception_occurred = None  # Initialize this BEFORE try block

try:
    run = mlflow.start_run()
    mlflow.set_tags({"kind": "index_creation", "component": "faq_knowledge_base"})
    mlflow.log_params(
        {
            "faq_index_name": settings.elastic.faq_index_name,
            "chat_sessions_index_name": settings.elastic.chat_sessions_index_name,
            "llm_cache_index_name": settings.elastic.llm_cache_index_name,
            "embedding_dimension": settings.elastic.embedding_dimension,
        }
    )
    logger.info(f"started index creation mlflow run: {run.info.run_id}")

    cluster_info = client.info()
    logger.info(
        f"connected to elasticsearch cluster '{cluster_info['cluster_name']}' "
        f"version {cluster_info['version']['number']}"
    )

    indices_to_create = {
        settings.elastic.faq_index_name: build_faq_index_mapping(
            settings.elastic.embedding_dimension
        ),
        settings.elastic.chat_sessions_index_name: build_chat_sessions_index_mapping(),
        settings.elastic.llm_cache_index_name: build_llm_cache_index_mapping(
            settings.elastic.embedding_dimension
        ),
    }

    created_count = 0
    for index_name, mapping in indices_to_create.items():
        if create_index(client=client, index_name=index_name, mapping=mapping):
            created_count += 1
    mlflow.log_metric("indices_created", created_count)

    mlflow_status = RunStatus.to_string(RunStatus.FINISHED)
    end_time = time.time()
    elapsed_time = end_time - start_time
    logger.info(f"finished index creation job in {elapsed_time:.2f} seconds")

except Exception as e:
    exception_occurred = e  # Save exception for re-raising
    mlflow_status = RunStatus.to_string(RunStatus.FAILED)
    mlflow.log_text(str(e), "error.log")
    logger.exception("index creation job failed", exc_info=e)

finally:
    mlflow.end_run(mlflow_status)
    logger.info("closed index creation mlflow run")

if exception_occurred:
    raise exception_occurred
